# Decisions

Design decisions made while working on the open issues, with the reason for each and where it stands. Newest first.

**Who decided:**
- **maintainer:** chosen in review.
- **principle:** settled by a numbered rule in `docs/Design Principles.rst`.
- **proposed:** made during implementation and not yet confirmed.

**Status:** `done` (built), `open` or `gap` (not built yet), `changed (date)` (the maintainer chose otherwise; the reason points to the decision that replaces it), `closed (date)` (not to be built), `superseded (date)` (replaced by a later fix).

**Before asking the maintainer, check the principles.** If one settles the question, apply it and record it here.

---

## 2026-11-13

### Recurrent fits from SurpyvalData (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Every recurrent model's `fit` takes a `SurpyvalData` with units `i` as `x` (decorator `accepts_recurrent_surpyval_data` in `surpyval/utils/recurrent_data.py`, one line per `fit`), turning the rows into the arrays `handle_xicn` takes and calling the same `fit`; the other data arguments are refused beside it. `EventHistoryData`, `handle_xicn` and `fit_from_recurrent_data` are unchanged. | maintainer (entry, one conversion), proposed (module, decorator) | One data model for every family; the fit is the fit of the arrays, so nothing can differ. | done |
| A unit's rows are in time order (the data checks it). A row starts at its `tl` where finite, else where the previous row ended. A finite `tl` after the previous row's `x` starts a new observation window (a gap): the data is given as `windows`, each window from its first row's start (0 where the unit has no entry) to its last row's `x`; a window's `c = 1` row is its close. Without gaps each unit's `tl` is its first row's `tl` (constant per unit, as `handle_xicn` needs). | proposed | `handle_xicn` reads a unit's entry once and gaps only as `windows`; the per-row stretches of `SurpyvalData` say both. | done |
| A `c = 1` row before a unit's last row, followed by a row from its `x`, is dropped when the covariates do not change there (the unit was watched throughout). Where `Z` changes within a unit, or `Z` comes with gaps, the fit is made by the model's `fit_tvc` from the rows as start-stop rows (`xl` the row's start, 0 for a unit with no entry); a model with no `fit_tvc` (`WeiLinWeissfeld`) refuses it, and so does finite `tr` there. | proposed | Covariate stretches are the TVC route's data; dropping an uninformative "still running" point is exact. | done |
| `a` does not change the arrays. A unit whose last row is an event with `a = 1` is fitted as an event followed by the end of its observation (no recurrent model models terminal events): today, a unit with no `c = 1` row is watched until its last event, which is the same thing. A last event with `a = 0` given and no `c = 1` row is read the same way (as `handle_xicn` reads it). Without `a` (one row per unit, `data.a` None) an event on a unit's last row is read as terminal, as `SurpyvalData` reads it elsewhere. | maintainer (terminal as end of observation), proposed (a = 0 and no-a readings) | Matches today's reading of the arrays exactly. | done |
| Fitted recurrent models carry `notes` (a tuple of text, empty from arrays), set by `fit(data)` and `fit_from_df(a_col=...)` and shown at the end of the `repr` under "Notes". Three notes: units ended in a terminal event; for how many of those `a` was not given; units ending at an event with `a = 0` and no end-of-record row. Not serialised (a note is about the data the model was fitted from). | proposed | Models had no notes mechanism; a tuple attribute and one repr line per model class is the smallest consistent one. | done |
| Single-event data (each unit one row) is fitted as the arrays fit it: each unit observed to its first event (or to its `c = 1` row), with the terminal note. | proposed | Legitimately "each unit observed to its first event". | done |
| Refused, saying what to use: data without `i` (by the existing `TypeError` of `coerce_xcnt_x`, unchanged except that it now names `SurpyvalData(x, c, ..., i=i)`); more than one state (the existing multi-state clause); markers (the existing message); weights; step time-varying covariates from records; covariates or event types the fit has no argument for (naming the models that take them); gaps for a fit without `windows` (renewal, cause-specific, Cox); truncation or counts a fit has no argument for. `handle_xicn`'s own refusals surface unchanged. | maintainer (refuse), proposed (wording) | Principles 2 and 22. | done |
| Every recurrent `fit_from_df` takes keyword `a_col` (decorator `takes_terminal_column`): read and checked as `SurpyvalData` reads `a` (after sorting each unit's rows as `handle_xicn` does; a refusal names the frame's rows); the fit is unchanged and the notes are added. | maintainer (`a_col`), proposed (details) | The same reading through every entry point. | done |
| Conformance: every recurrent case has a `"fit(data)"` path, the fixture as a `SurpyvalData` (`_with_data_path` in `registry_cases.py`), compared with `fit` by `test_fit_paths_agree`. | proposed | The suite checks the entry for every model. | done |
| After merging with the data model: data holding measurements (`m`, one or several) is refused by every recurrent fit with the existing markers message, checked on `data.m`; a range row (`xl`, `xr` given with `x`) is fitted as the arrays' interval row, except where covariates change within a unit (the `fit_tvc` route reads a row from its unit's previous row), where it is refused. | proposed | `data.markers` refuses data with several measurements with a message about reading one, which said nothing about recurrent fits. A start-stop row has no place for a range starting after the previous row ended. | done |
| `JointFrailty` (the one recurrent model of a terminal event) reads `a` as its terminal event (decorator `accepts_terminal_surpyval_data` in `surpyval/utils/recurrent_data.py`): a unit's events with `a = 0` are its recurrent events (`c = 0` rows), its event with `a = 1` its terminal event (the closing row `c = 1`, `terminal = 1` of the arrays, not also a recurrent event: a recurrent event that ended the unit is two rows at that age, `a = 0` then `a = 1`), and a last `c = 1` row the end of its record without it (`terminal = 0`); a `c = 1` row before the last is dropped (watched throughout); the entry is the first row's `tl`. The fit is that of those arrays (tested equal: parameters, covariance, standard errors, `mcf`, `sf`, frailties, `to_dict`). A last event with `a = 0` given and no `c = 1` row becomes that event plus a closing row at its age with `terminal = 0`. | proposed | The terminal flag exists for this model; the arrays' closing row with `terminal` and the data model's event with `a = 1` say the same thing. | done |
| `JointFrailty`'s notes: where `a` was inferred on a unit's last event, a note says it was read as the unit's terminal event (fitted as such, not as a recurrent event) and to give `a = 0`, with a `c = 1` row at the end of the record, where it was an ordinary event; a note for a last event with `a = 0` and no end of record. It never gets the "does not model terminal events" note. `JointFrailtyModel` gained `notes` and shows them in its `repr` (not serialised). | proposed | An inferred `a = 1` changes this model's fit (unlike the others'), so it must be visible. | done |
| `JointFrailty` refuses in words, saying what to use: data without `i`, states, measurements, weights (as every recurrent fit), event types, ranges and left- or interval-censored rows, counts, gaps in observation, covariates that change within a unit, right truncation; and `Z`, `i`, `c`, `terminal`, `tl` beside the data. `fit_from_df` takes keyword `a_col` in place of `terminal_col` (now optional; exactly one of the two): the table is put in each unit's time order (at one age: recurrent events, then the terminal event or the end of the record), `tl_col` read as the entry on the unit's first row, and fitted as `fit(data)`. Its conformance case has `"fit(data)"` and `"fit_from_df(a_col)"` paths (`_joint_as_data`, `_joint_as_frame`). | proposed | The model's own limits (see "Joint frailty model"), stated before the arrays' checks; `a_col` as on every recurrent `fit_from_df`, here with the meaning that changes the fit. | done |
| `PanelCountMCF` and `PanelCountRegression` refuse a `SurpyvalData` (`TypeError`), saying they take arrays of visits and counts (`fit`, `fit_from_df`) and that `NonParametricCounting.fit(data)` and the other recurrent models take event times. The refusal decorator is now generic, `refuses_surpyval_data_with(message)` in `surpyval/utils/surpyval_data.py`; the panel multi-state models use it with their own message (unchanged). | proposed | A panel row is an inspection with the count found since the previous one, including 0; a `SurpyvalData`'s rows are events or stretches and its `n` (identical units, at least 1) cannot be 0, so an inspection that found nothing has no row, and reading rows as visits would be a second meaning of `n`. | done |

### Degradation and joint fits from Measurements (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The degradation fitters whose input is repeated readings per unit (`DegradationAnalysis`, `WienerProcess`, `GammaProcess`) take a `Measurements`, or a `SurpyvalData` holding `m`, as their first argument, in place of `x`, `y` and `i` (`fit(m, threshold=...)`): the spelling of `Weibull.fit(data)`, not a `fit_from_surpyval_data`. `y`, `i` and `threshold` now default to None in the signature; with arrays, a missing one is a TypeError naming it, as Python's was. | maintainer (entry from a data object), proposed (first-argument spelling, defaults) | The family's fits already take their data first; one spelling across the three. A separate method would double the surface. | done |
| The readings become one row per reading, grouped by unit (the Measurements' unit order, or the data's row order) and in time order within a unit: the order of the long arrays users give today, so the fit, `to_dict` included, is the arrays' exactly (tested). | proposed | The fits sort units and times themselves, so any order gives the same numbers to rounding; this one gives them to the bit. | done |
| From a `SurpyvalData`, the data's covariates `Z` (one row per unit) are the unit's stress, repeated on each of its readings, for every mode (`links`, `acceleration="clock"`, the process models' stress); named covariates name the stress as `fit_from_df`'s `Z_cols` do (`model.Z_cols`). The life rows' times and censoring are not used: the models define failure as the path reaching `threshold`, and today's arguments take no life times. Counts, weights, truncation, event types, time-varying covariates, states and units of several rows are refused through `lost_message`. `y`, `i` or `Z` given beside a data object is a TypeError; a stress that changes during a unit's test stays an array input. | proposed | Per-unit stress is the one natural, unambiguous mapping; the readings' internal order is the package's, so a per-reading `Z` beside them could not be aligned by the user. | done |
| A unit with no readings (in the life rows, or listed with `{}` in a Measurements) is left out with one warning naming it. Readings of a unit with no life row are not used (warned of when attached, as for the joint models). | proposed | Arrays have no such unit; dropping it silently is not allowed (principle 22). | done |
| Several measurements are refused, naming them and `m.with_measurement(name)` (`data.with_measurement(name)` for data). `Measurements.with_measurement(name)` is added (public), rather than a `measurement=` argument on every fit. | proposed | One way to choose, the data model's, on both objects; no new fit arguments. | done |
| `fit_from_df` of the three names the readings' column `m_col`, with `y_col` its other name (alias rule: kept, undeprecated, never different); both given is a TypeError; the default is still `"y"` (`y_col` now defaults to None, read as `"y"`). `x_col` and `i_col` already had the names. | maintainer | The readings' column has `Measurements.from_dataframe`'s name. | done |
| `DestructiveDegradation` is not wired: one reading per unit, readings censored by their own `c`, no unit ids; a `Measurements` has no censoring, and its units add nothing there. Its arrays and `fit_from_df` are unchanged (no `m_col`). | proposed | Not a natural mapping. | done |
| The joint fit takes a `Measurements` in `markers`, or in `m` (its other name: alias rule; both given is a TypeError), and a new `i` (each row's unit). Units are matched by id, never position: a row whose unit has no readings gets `{}` (as from a `SurpyvalData`, silently, an item fitted on its lifetime alone); readings of a unit with no row are warned of through the same `attached` warning as `SurpyvalData(m=...)` and not used; a unit on several rows is refused. `i` with a list of markers is refused (the list is per row). A `Measurements` beside a `SurpyvalData` without readings is attached to it (`data.m`) and fitted as data holding it; beside data that holds readings, `markers`/`m` is refused, naming the one given. | maintainer (two inputs, by id, `markers` kept), proposed (`m` alias, `i`, the rest) | The existing data path's rules, so a fit is the same whichever way the readings come. | done |
| The joint `fit_from_df` takes `i_col` (the items' units) and `m` (a `Measurements`, from the long readings frame by `Measurements.from_dataframe`), as an alternative to `markers_col`; both is a TypeError. Not `x_col`/`m_col` of a second frame: `x_col` already names the life ages there. | proposed | Two frames cannot share one `x_col`; building the Measurements is one call with the readings' own column names. | done |
| Conformance: `DegradationAnalysis[linear]`, `[power]`, `WienerProcess`, `GammaProcess` and the four `WeibullJointPH` cases declare the paths `fit(measurements)` and `fit(data)`; `DestructiveDegradation` and `InducedFailureDistribution` (built from a `DegradationAnalysis` fit) declare none. `surpyval.Measurements` is listed out of scope as data (it was public and unlisted, failing `test_completeness`). | proposed | As other families declare their alternate entries. | done |

### Several rows per unit and the terminal flag a (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SurpyvalData` takes `a` (constructor `a=`, `from_dataframe(a_col=)`, `data.a`): 1 where the row's event ended the unit's history for good, 0 elsewhere. Not `d`, which the `xrd` format uses. The same idea as entering an absorbing state. | maintainer | One data model for life data with time-varying covariates, recurrent events and both ending in a terminal event. | done |
| A unit may have several rows without `s` (the rule is still the one helper, `check_unit_ids`, now with no "each once"). Its rows are stretches `(tl, x]` of the unit's own clock, in the order given: the four patterns (stretches then a terminal event; events with `a = 0` then more; events then a terminal event; a last row still running). | maintainer | Time-varying covariates and recurrent events are both a unit's rows on its own clock. | done |
| Rows of a unit are not reordered: a row whose `x` is before the previous row's end, or whose `tl` is before it, is refused naming the rows ("out of order" and "overlap" are one check). Equal `x` is allowed only after an event (simultaneous events, or the end of the record at an event's time), as `handle_xicn` takes them (it repeats an exact event's row for simultaneous events and puts an end of observation after an event at the same time); after a `c = 1` row an equal `x` is a stretch of no length and refused. | proposed | Reordering silently would hide a mistake in a unit's history, and "a row after a terminal event" needs an order. | done |
| An event row (`c` other than 1) with no `a` (or a blank cell) is terminal (1) on its unit's last row, 0 on an earlier one; `data.a_inferred` (a boolean per row, True where the rule filled `a`) records it for the fits' notes. An `a` given always wins. `a` holds 0, 1, True, False or a blank; anything else is refused. | maintainer (rule, explicit wins), proposed (`a_inferred`) | Small, per row, survives subsetting and JSON. | done |
| On a `c = 1` row only 0 or blank is accepted (stored 0); `a = 1` is refused naming the rows. A row after an `a = 1` row of the same unit is refused naming the rows. | maintainer (refusals), proposed (0/blank only) | A running row has no event to end anything. | done |
| `data.a` is None, and everything is as before, where `a` is not given and no unit has more than one row. With `s`, `a` is not inferred (None unless given) and, when given, is validated only. | maintainer (None case), proposed (no inference with `s`) | Step 1's data with states repeats ids; inferring `a` there would change its repr and dict, and the transitions say which states are absorbing. | done |
| `a` (and `a_inferred`) carried by indexing, `select`/`drop`/every reshape (a row a reshape censors gets `a = 0`), `to_dataframe` (column `a`; `from_dataframe(a_col=)` reads it back as given, so `a_inferred` is not kept through a table), `to_dict`/`from_dict`/JSON (`"a"`, `"a_inferred"`, stamped `"schema": 4` as states are, since an older reader would take a unit's rows as units), the repr (column `a` only when present) and `summary()` ("units: N (k ended for good, m still running)", a unit ended for good where its last row has `a = 1`). | maintainer (carry everywhere), proposed (details) | As `s` is carried (step 1). | done |
| `n_items` counts distinct units wherever ids repeat (each by the `n` of its first row), generalising step 1's rule for `s`. | maintainer | A unit with several rows is one unit. | done |
| Fits of one row per unit refuse data without `s` whose units have several rows, through the "lost" message, in a clause of its own naming the units and the kind: "repeated events ... the recurrent models in surpyval.recurrent take them" where any event has `a = 0`, else "stretches with the covariates fixed in each; the model's fit_tvc takes them, fit_tvc(i=data.i, xl=data.tl, xr=data.x, c=data.c, Z=data.Z)". So do `fit_from_surpyval_data`, `to_xrd`, `life_table` and the Bayesian and PyMC likelihoods (through `refuse_multi_state`). A fit with an `a` argument is given `data.a`, and `data.i` where it has `i`, and is not refused. | maintainer (refuse, say the kind), proposed (wording, detection) | Fitting a unit's rows as independent units would count it several times. | done |
| `item(id)`, `to_items()` and `records()` refuse data whose units have several rows, and data whose `a` says something a record cannot (an event with `a = 0`); `update` refuses data with `a`. | proposed | As step 1 did for `s`: an `ItemLifetime` is one unit's single row. | done |
| Without `s`, `e` keeps today's behaviour on such data, so an event with no `e` is still warned of as a masked cause. | maintainer | `e` keeps its meaning without states; the model step decides what an untyped repair means. | done |

### Exact times and ranges together: x, xl and xr (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `x`, `xl` and `xr` may be given together and are read row by row: an exact time in `x` with `xl` and `xr` blank (None, NaN, pandas `NA`), or a range in `xl` and `xr` with `x` blank, read by today's rules for a `[xl, xr]` row (`(v, inf)` right censored, `(-inf, v)` left censored, `(v1, v2)` interval, `xl == xr` exact). `(0, v)` stays an interval. Only `x`, or only `xl`/`xr`, runs exactly the code it did. | maintainer | A table with a column of exact times and two of ranges is common; the reader does not know the distribution's support, so it must not guess left censoring from a 0. | done |
| A row giving both an exact time and a range (`x` and either end) is left out of every per-row argument (`c`, `n`, `t`/`tl`/`tr`, `Z`, `e`, `s`, `i`, `w`) with a `UserWarning` naming how many and which rows and what to do; never reconciled. All rows left out is refused. | maintainer | Which of the two holds is not known; principle 22's warning. | done |
| A row with no time, and a range with one end blank (with `x` blank), are refused naming the rows; except in `SurpyvalData.from_dataframe` and `ParametricCompetingRisks.fit_from_df`, which already read a blank `xl`/`xr` cell as `-inf`/`inf` alone and so do here. | maintainer (no time), proposed (one end) | Match each entry point's existing reading of a blank end: the constructor, the handler and the other `fit_from_df`s refuse a NaN in `xl`/`xr` today. | done |
| A `c` given must agree with each range row's reading (2 is also taken on any range with different ends, which is how a `[xl, xr]` row is flagged today), else refused naming the rows and both readings. On a range with equal ends `c` is the user's, as it is for `[v, v]` today. A blank `c` cell on a range row is read from the bounds; on an exact row it is refused, as is `c = 2` with one exact time. | maintainer (refuse a disagreement), proposed (the rest) | The bounds decide where `c` says nothing; an exact row has no bounds to read. | done |
| One reader, `data_formats.exact_or_range` (returns the `[lower, upper]` rows, the flags and the rows kept), with `drop_rows` for the other per-row arguments; the DataFrame entry points use `dataframe.exact_or_range_frame`, which drops the rows from the frame and writes the bounds into `xl_col`/`xr_col` (and the flags into `c_col`), so each `fit_from_df` then reads the pure `xl`/`xr` form. | proposed | One place for the rule; the fits see exactly the data the pure form gives, so the results are bit-identical (tested). | done |
| Where "x or xl/xr, not both" was enforced: `xcnt_handler` (via `SurpyvalData`), `SurpyvalData.from_dataframe`, the shared `UnivariateDataFrameMixin.fit_from_df` and `RegressionDataFrameMixin.fit_from_df` (trees, forest), `DataFrameRegressionMixin.fit_from_df` (parametric regressions, distributional regression), the Bayesian `fit_bayes_from_df` and `ParametricCompetingRisks.fit_from_df` now take the three. Left refusing: the joint models' `fit_from_df` (`univariate/joint`, another agent's area); the recurrent and time-varying-covariate `fit_from_df`s and `EventHistoryData.from_dataframe`, where `xl`/`xr` are a stretch's start and stop, not a range of one time; the copula's `xl_cols`/`xr_cols` (two series per row). | proposed | Only where `xl`/`xr` mean the range of one time does the reading apply. | done |
| `lost_message` says a shape the data has in a clause of its own: "This fit takes no event types, which the SurpyvalData holds, and takes data in one state only (this data's rows are in more than one, s)." | maintainer | The old wording ("takes no event types or more than one state (s)") did not read. | done |

### Measurements in the data model: m (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Readings taken on a unit over its life are a separate object, `Measurements` (public, `surpyval.Measurements`), linked to the life rows by the unit `i`, never mixed into event rows. Several named measurements may be kept together; they need not share times or units. `SurpyvalData(..., m=...)` and `from_dataframe(..., m=...)` attach one (`data.m`); readings are a frame of their own (`Measurements.from_dataframe`), not columns of the life frame. | maintainer | Measurements happen independently of the life events, on their own timeline. | done |
| `data.m` and `data.markers` are one store: the dataset keeps a `Measurements`; `markers` is a property returning the one measurement's `{i: {x: value}}` (the store's own dict), and setting it stores that dict, unchecked, as one unnamed measurement. Neither name is deprecated. With several measurements, reading `data.markers` is refused, naming `data.m[name]` and `data.with_measurement(name)`; with one named measurement it returns its readings. | maintainer (one store, neither deprecated), proposed (several refused, one named returned) | The house rule for an alias ("Distribution and life-model aliases (12 Nov 2026)"). Every existing reader of `markers` (the joint fits, `from_items`, reshapes, `to_dict`) gets exactly what it got; refusing where there are several stops a reader silently picking one. Old pickles holding `markers` load through the property. | done |
| "Plain" measurements (one unnamed measurement with no unit label, what `from_items` and `markers` give) print, save and reshape by the old code paths, byte for byte: the `markers` column and summary line, `"markers"` in `to_dict` with no schema stamp. Named measurements, or one with a unit label, show an `m` column and a summary line `m: vibration, temperature - 27 units, 412 readings` (ASCII, as the other reprs), and are written under `"m"` (`Measurements.to_dict()`), stamped `"schema": 4` (`serialisation._has_measurements`). | proposed | Old files load and new files of the old shape stay readable by old readers; a v0.26 reader refuses data whose readings it would silently drop, the rule step 1 used for `"s"`. | done |
| The two dict shapes are told apart by what the innermost dicts hold: numbers are readings (`{i: {x: value}}`), dicts are units' readings (`{name: {i: {x: value}}}`). A dict with no reading at all (so neither) and one mixing the two are refused; `name=` with the named shape is refused. No extra constructors: the constructor, `from_dataframe` and `from_dict`. | proposed | The shape is structural, so nothing is guessed; the one undecidable case holds nothing anyway. A small surface. | done |
| Validation: times are finite numbers of any sign (not text, not booleans), values finite numbers, one reading per unit, measurement and time (a DataFrame's duplicate is refused naming both rows), unit ids present, hashable and of one kind (`check_item_ids`), names text or numbers. No censoring or counts on readings (no argument for them). Readings are not checked against the unit's observation window. | proposed | Not stricter than the models: the joint models check each reading against the unit's observation (through `ItemLifetime`) when they fit, and the clock may start below 0 (left truncation below 0); degradation data asks only for finite times. | done |
| Unit labels go through `normalise_unit` (pint's canonical name: `"mm/s"` is stored as `"millimeter / second"`). | proposed | The package's one unit-label rule; keeping the label as typed would be a second rule. | done |
| `m=` needs `i` (refused otherwise, naming `i=`); a unit in `m` with no life rows is warned of (count and first ids) and kept. `data.markers = ...` keeps its old, unchecked behaviour (rows may be keyed by row number where there are no ids). | maintainer | Measurements are linked by unit; dropping readings silently is not allowed. | done |
| Subsetting and reshaping keep the readings of the units kept. For plain measurements the old code runs unchanged. For named ones the record route carries no markers (an `ItemLifetime` holds one unnamed measurement) and the dataset puts its `m` back for the units kept; a unit whose observation window a reshape moved (`censor_at`, `enter_at`, `for_event_type`, `extend_running`) keeps the readings inside its new window, as markers do; a unit whose window did not move keeps every reading. `to_items`, `item` and `records` refuse named measurements, naming `without("m")` and `m.for_unit(i)`; `update` refuses records with markers into data with named measurements. | proposed | The markers' semantics, without dropping readings given outside a window no reshape touched. | done |
| `without("m")` and `without("measurements")` are the same part as `without("markers")`. `with_measurement(name)` keeps one measurement. A fit with a `markers` argument refuses data with several measurements, naming `with_measurement(name)` and `without("m")`; other fits refuse measurements with the markers' message. Joint fits from `m=` data equal those from the old markers (tested bit for bit). | maintainer (synonyms, subsetting method), proposed (wording) | The joint models take one marker; an argument to choose it is a later step's. | done |
| With several rows per unit (stays in states, stretches, repeated events with `a`), `m` stays per unit: the "no life rows" check is by distinct unit; a unit's readings are kept while any of its rows is kept; a reshape's window for a unit is the span of its rows; the table shows a unit's reading count on its first row and blank on its later rows (the `markers` column too, which is unchanged for one row per unit); `to_dict` of plain markers still writes one entry per row (a unit's repeated, read back by id). A fit with a `markers` argument refuses units of several rows in `_markers_taken`, before it could be handed a unit's readings once per row: with the one-row-per-unit message of `several_rows_clause` where there are no states (the same text the fit's own refusal gives), else a message of its own. Rows the x/xl/xr rule leaves out are dropped before `m` is attached, so a unit whose only row went is warned of as a unit with no life rows, after the warning about the row. | proposed | Readings are taken on the unit; counting them once per row would weigh a unit by its number of rows. | done |
| A reshape through records (`without`, `select`, `censor_at`, `enter_at`, `extend_running`, `for_event_type`, ...) carries the readings in each record's markers only where every unit has one row and the data has no `s` or `a`; otherwise the records carry none and the dataset puts `m` back by unit (trimmed only where a unit's span of rows moved), and `without("m")` drops it without going through records. Before, a unit's readings were checked against each of its rows' windows and refused. A record reshape of measurements whose data has no unit ids is refused, naming `i=` and `without('m')`. | proposed | A unit's readings belong to the unit, not to one of its rows. | done |
| `extend_running` and `update` chose the record type by `_on_arrays`, so data with marker readings (which reshapes through records) was taken for event histories and failed (`'ItemLifetime' object has no attribute '_end'`; `update takes ItemHistory records`); they now ask whether the data is lifetime data. | proposed | A fault found while carrying `m` through every reshape. | done |

### Multi-state fits from SurpyvalData (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The four multi-state fits (`MultiState`, `MultiStateProportionalHazards`, `ParametricMultiState`, `ParametricMultiStateRegression`) take a `SurpyvalData` as `x`: `x`, `e`, `s` (as `state`), `tl`, `i`, `n`, `Z` (named by `covariate_names`) and the unit come from the data, and the other data arguments must be left out; the fit equals the fit from the arrays exactly (tested: estimates, covariance, occupancy and its variances, `to_dict`). Through `accepts_surpyval_data`, as the other families, with a small decorator of their own (`multistate._entry.multistate_data`) above it for what only these models say. | maintainer (design), proposed (the decorator) | One route for every family; the extra checks need the transitions and the model's name. The regression's names come by giving the fit the covariates as a named table, the way its arrays fit already reads names. | done |
| Data without `s` starts every row from one state: the one state of `transitions` with no transition into it, or `"event-free"` without `transitions` (the name `TransitionMatrix.competing_risks` gives it); several such states are refused, asking for `s`. A failure with no state in `e` (a masked cause), and data with no `e` (plain survival), are refused, naming KaplanMeier / CompetingRisks. Equal to `CompetingRisks` and `ParametricCompetingRisks` to the tolerances of the array identities. | maintainer (one starting state, the rule for the label), proposed (refusing e-less data) | A state for "failed" would be a label the user never gave; the survival fitters already take such data. | done |
| Refused, with the rows and the fix: `c` of -1 or 2 (a change of state seen only between two times is not supported by these models), right truncation, time-varying covariates, covariates given to a model without them (naming the two regressions); weights through the existing "lost" message. `state` or `s` beside a `SurpyvalData` is refused even when the data has no `s` (where `accepts_surpyval_data` would let an argument the data lacks through). | proposed | The generic "lost" message would advise `data.without('truncation')` (which drops the entry times too), `fit_tvc` or a univariate regression, none of which applies here. The states belong in the data. | done |
| `s` is another name for `state` in `fit`, and `s_col` for `state_col` in `fit_from_df`, neither deprecated; both given is a `TypeError`. One decorator (`multistate._entry.other_name`) adds the keyword to the signature; positional calls are unchanged. `state` and `state_col` keep their place and stay required in the shown signature. | maintainer (names), proposed (decorator) | The rule of "Distribution and life-model aliases (12 Nov 2026)". A keyword-only alias leaves the method bodies (and their positional order) as they were. | done |
| No `c` argument on the multi-state `fit`s. | proposed (maintainer's direction) | `e` already says whether each row moved; a second flag would only be another thing to disagree. `fit(data)` reads the data's `c`, which `SurpyvalData` has already checked against `e`. | done |
| With the terminal flag `a` (and `s`), the multi-state fits ignore `a`: the transitions say which states absorb, and a terminal event is the same idea as entering one. `fit(data)` with an `a` column equals the fit without (tested). | maintainer | `a` is stored and validated by the data; deriving absorbing states from it would be a second source for what `transitions` says. | done |
| Data without `s` whose units have several rows (stretches, or repeated events now that the data model allows them) is refused, naming the units, the reading (repeated events: the recurrent models; stretches: a regression's `fit_tvc`) and `s` for a multi-state model; data without `s` and one row per unit is still the one-starting-state case. Measurements (`m`, `markers`) are refused by the existing markers message of `accepts_surpyval_data` (the pre-check does not bypass it). Rows a reading of `x`/`xl`/`xr` left out with a warning are simply not there: the fit is that of the rows kept (tested). Data whose times are held as `[lower, upper]` (exact times and ranges together) is fitted on each row's lower end once only exact and right-censored rows remain (the exact time, or the time last seen). | maintainer (refuse several rows), proposed (wording) | Fitting several rows of a unit as separate units starting afresh would be another model, silently. | done |
| Conformance: each multi-state case registers a `fit(data)` path (a `SurpyvalData` with `s`), compared with `fit` by `test_fit_paths` and the other suites that read `Case.paths`. | proposed | Where the suite registers every model's other entry points. | done |
| The panel models (`PanelMultiState`, `PanelMultiStateRegression`) are not wired to a `SurpyvalData`: `fit(data)` is a `TypeError` saying they take arrays of visits. | proposed | Their `s` is the state seen at a visit; a `SurpyvalData`'s `s` is the state during the row, so reading one as the other would fit another model. | done |

### State in the data model: x, c, n, t, i, e, s, Z (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SurpyvalData` takes `s`, the state the unit was in during the row (from its entry `tl` to `x`); `e` is the state it moved into at `x`; `c` says whether it moved (0) or was still in `s` (1); `i` is the unit the row belongs to. | maintainer | One data model for survival, competing-risks and multi-state data, in the long form (one row per stay) the multi-state models already take. | done |
| Without `s` every row starts from the one initial state: ordinary survival data and competing risks (the failure mode is the state entered) are the one-starting-state case, and everything behaves exactly as before (reprs, dicts, warnings, fits; checked against values recorded before the change and by the refactor snapshot). | maintainer | Nothing that exists changes meaning. | done |
| `ids` becomes `i`; `ids` stays as its other name, undeprecated and never behaving differently: one constructor argument (giving both is a `TypeError`), one list (`data.i is data.ids`; setting either sets both). The dictionary keeps the key `"ids"`, so files load both ways. | maintainer (name and alias), proposed (storage) | The rule of "Distribution and life-model aliases (12 Nov 2026)": an alias is kept, undeprecated, as long as it never behaves differently. `i` is a property over the stored `ids`, so pickles of older data keep their ids. `EventHistoryData` keeps its own meanings (`i` per row, `ids` the distinct units); on `SurpyvalData` both are per row. | done |
| Without `s` each id is once per row, as before. With `s` an id may repeat (a unit's stays) but ids are still of one kind; `n_items` counts the distinct units (each by the `n` of its first row; without ids each row is a unit); `select(ids=...)` and `drop` take all of a unit's rows. The rule is one helper (`check_unit_ids`), and the lookup of a unit's rows another (`data_reshape.rows_of_units`). | maintainer (repeats only with `s`), proposed (`n_items`, helpers) | A unit with several stays is still one unit. One place each, so a later step that allows several rows per unit without `s` changes one helper. | done |
| `item(id)`, `to_items()` and `records()` refuse data with `s`, naming `to_dataframe()`, `select(ids=...)` and `data[rows]`. | proposed | An `ItemLifetime` has no place for a state, and a unit with several stays would be several records under one id; dropping the state silently is not allowed. | done |
| With `s`, `e` and `c` must agree: `c` left out is read from `e` (blank: 1; a state: 0); `c` given with `e` is checked row by row (`c == 1` with a state in `e`, or any other `c` with a blank `e`, is refused, naming the rows and the fix). With `s` and `c` but no `e`, `c` is used as given (a move with no state named, as in survival data); with neither, refused. Without `s`, `e` keeps its warnings (a suspension's type dropped, masked causes). | maintainer (strict only with `s`; masked causes unchanged without it), proposed (`c` without `e`) | A disagreement is a mistake in the data; guessing which of the two is right would hide it. Masked causes are a competing-risks feature and stay. A reshape that drops `e` (`without("event_types")`, `for_event_type`) leaves data whose moves are the event, which must stay buildable. | done |
| With `s` and no `c`, a range row (`xl`, `xr`) takes its flag from its bounds, not from `e`: `(v, inf)` 1 (written as the time `v`, flagged 1), `(-inf, v)` -1 (written as `v`), `(v1, v2)` 2; an exact row's flag is read from `e` as before; then `e` must agree as for a `c` given (refused naming the rows). Rows left out by the x/xl/xr rule are dropped before, so their `e`, `s` and `i` are not consulted. `xl`/`xr` without `x` take the same row-by-row reading when `s` is given and `c` is not. A `c` given is used as it is. | proposed | Reading `e` first made every range a blank or a state and then contradicted the range (a right-censored range read as 1 met the handler's 2 for a two-ended row); the range says what is known, `e` must agree with it. | done |
| `s` holds categories: a 1-D object array of the plain values given (`1` stays an int, `"1"` text, never floats); a missing state, a single value, an unhashable value or a wrong length is refused. Giving `s` turns `group_and_sort` off. No `states` property. | maintainer (grouping off), proposed (storage, no `states`) | States are labels, as event types are. `EventHistoryData.states` already means each item's state history, so a list of distinct states under the same name would mean something else; the multi-state fits work it out. | done |
| `from_dataframe(s_col=)`; where the states column is whole numbers, the states entered (`e_col`, which pandas stores as floats once it has a blank) are read back as whole numbers. | proposed | Otherwise `2.0` in `e` and `2` in `s` print differently for the same state. | done |
| Everything that subsets or reshapes carries `s` and `i` by row: `data[rows]`, `select`, `drop`, `groupby`, `without`, `with_covariates`, `for_event_type`, `censor_at`, `enter_at`, `extend_running`; `relabel_event_types` renames a state in `s` and `e` alike; `without("states")` drops `s` (the slot `EventHistoryData` already has); `update` refuses data with `s`. The reshapes that go through records number them by row and put the ids and states back by row. | proposed | A reshape that lost the states would turn multi-state data into pooled data. `update` replaces a record by its id, which a unit with several rows cannot mean. | done |
| `to_xrd` (and `life_table`, `log_likelihood_fn`, the PyMC data) refuse data in more than one state, naming the multi-state models and `data[data.s == state]`; a single state passes through as if there were no `s`. `to_xcnt` is unchanged (it returns the arrays; the states are `data.s`, as covariates are `data.Z`). `to_observations` refuses `s`. | maintainer (`to_xrd`, one state as none), proposed (the rest) | A risk set over stays in different states is another model. `to_xcnt` does not pool anything; `from_observations` has no place for a state. | done |
| Fits that are not multi-state refuse data in more than one state through the "lost" message (`fit(data)`, `fit_from_surpyval_data`, the Bayesian fits); data in one state fits as if it had no `s` (bit-identical). A fit with a `state` (or `s`) argument is given `data.s`, and `data.i` as `i` where it has one; without a `c` argument its 0/1 flags are carried by `e`. | maintainer (refusal, one state as none), proposed (filling `state` and `i`) | The multi-state fits can then take `fit(data)` without a decorator of their own. | done |
| `to_dict` writes `"s"` and stamps data with states `"schema": 4` (no new version: 4 is the unreleased v0.27 layout). | proposed | The file's rule for a new optional field an older reader would silently drop (as weights were stamped 3): a v0.26 reader refuses it rather than read every state's stays as one sample. | done |
| `fit_from_df`: none of the package's 37 builds a `SurpyvalData`; each reads its columns into the arrays of its own `fit`. `SurpyvalData.from_dataframe` takes `s_col` (it already took `i_col`). No `s_col` was added to a `fit_from_df` whose `fit` cannot take states; the multi-state ones already take `state_col` and `i_col`. | proposed | An `s_col` on a univariate or regression fit could only accept one state, a dead argument. The multi-state `fit_from_df`s will take `s_col` as another name for `state_col` in the next step (maintainer). | done (`from_dataframe`; multi-state `s_col`: see "Multi-state fits from SurpyvalData (13 Nov 2026)") |

| `fit_from_df`: none of the package's 37 builds a `SurpyvalData`; each reads its columns into the arrays of its own `fit`. `SurpyvalData.from_dataframe` takes `s_col` (it already took `i_col`). No `s_col` was added to a `fit_from_df` whose `fit` cannot take states; the multi-state ones already take `state_col` and `i_col`. | proposed | An `s_col` on a univariate or regression fit could only accept one state, a dead argument. The multi-state `fit_from_df`s will take `s_col` as another name for `state_col` in the next step (maintainer). | done (`from_dataframe`); open (multi-state `s_col`, next step) |
### Panel counts: recurrent events counted at inspections (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Two classes in a new module, `surpyval.recurrent.panel_count`: `PanelCountMCF` (the mean cumulative function, no covariates) and `PanelCountRegression` (the proportional means model `E[N(t) | Z] = Lambda0(t) exp(beta'Z)`), also at the top level. `fit(x, n, i=None, how=...)` and `fit(x, Z, n, i=None, how=..., init=None)`, `fit_from_df` with `x_col`, `n_col`, `i_col` (and `Z_cols`): one row per inspection, `n` the count since the unit's previous inspection (or since 0), 0 allowed. | proposed | The recurrent data conventions (`x`, `i`, `n`), with `n` read as the count found; a separate class rather than an option of `NonParametricCounting`, whose data (event times with `c`) and variance (Lawless-Nadeau) do not apply. | done |
| Every unit's counts start at time 0: no `tl` (late entry) and no `c`. A unit inspected twice at one time, a count that is negative or missing, and covariates that change between a unit's rows are refused, each saying what to do. | proposed | The pseudo-likelihood needs each unit's cumulative count from 0; late entry and time-varying covariates are not in the published estimators. | done |
| `PanelCountMCF` default `how="likelihood"`, the non-parametric maximum likelihood estimator under a Poisson working model (Wellner and Zhang 2000); `how="pseudo"` is the isotonic regression of the cumulative counts (Sun and Kalbfleisch 1995). | proposed | The likelihood estimator is the more efficient when inspection times differ between units (Wellner and Zhang's comparison); the pseudo-likelihood is exact and fast and is kept. | done |
| `PanelCountRegression` default `how="pseudo"` (Wellner and Zhang 2007's maximum pseudo-likelihood, `spef`'s `"MPL"`); `how="likelihood"` maximises the full likelihood over a step-function baseline. | proposed | The pseudo-likelihood profile in `beta` is concave with an exact baseline for each `beta` (a weighted isotonic regression), so the fit is fast and reproducible, and it has an R reference: spef `MPL` run with `absTol = relTol = 1e-12` (at its default tolerances the two optimisers stop at slightly different points, so they are compared at the tighter setting) agrees to 6.5e-9 in the coefficients and 1.1e-9 in the baseline on the simulated fixture. The likelihood fit has no exact R counterpart (spef's `MLs` uses a spline baseline; within 0.002 on the fixture). | done |
| The likelihood estimate is computed on the times where the likelihood reads the mean function (the ends of the intervals with a positive count, the inspection before each, and each unit's last inspection) and carried as a step function between them. | proposed | At any other inspection time the likelihood does not determine the mean function (every value between its neighbours is as likely: two runs from different starts differed by 0.035 there), so an unreduced estimate depended on the algorithm's path. The convention is the smallest non-decreasing curve consistent with the maximum. | done |
| The likelihood maximum: self-consistency (EM) steps accelerated by SQUAREM, then up to 20 iterative convex minorant steps (whose isotonic projection finds the zero increments), then projected Newton steps on the increments (Bertsekas's epsilon-active set; a dense Hessian built by 2-D cumulative sums up to 2000 times, conjugate gradients beyond), with the minorant algorithm to the end as a fallback; converged when the Karush-Kuhn-Tucker gap (the largest departure of a positive increment's ratio from 1, or of any ratio above 1) is below 1e-10 (1e-6 where the likelihood can no longer rise in floating point), and a `PanelCountConvergenceWarning` otherwise. A bootstrap resample's refit starts from the full data's estimate and stops at a gap of 1e-7. The regression alternates this with Newton steps for `beta`, then Newton on both together near the maximum. | proposed | The likelihood in the increments is concave, so a KKT point is the maximum (principle 13); EM alone took minutes on 30000 inspection times. | done |
| Bounds: percentile bootstraps over units (`mcf_cb`, `param_cb`; `param_cb(method="normal")` uses the bootstrap standard error), `standard_errors` the bootstrap spread; no model-based covariance, no `covariance()`. A seed caches the resamples. | proposed | The Poisson working model's information would be wrong for counts that are not Poisson; spef gives bootstrap standard errors for these methods. A sandwich variance for the profile estimators needs the projection of Wellner and Zhang 2007 and is left as a gap. | gap (sandwich) |
| With irregular (continuous) inspection times the MCF at a fixed time converges at the cube-root rate, where the bootstrap is not known to be valid; the docs say so, and the calibration checks that cell like the others. | proposed | Kosorok (2008) on bootstraps of isotonic estimators; with a finite set of inspection times the rate is the usual square root. Calibration (mixed Poisson, gamma frailty variance 0.5, n = 150, 300 replicates, 95% percentile bootstrap with 200 resamples): MCF at t = 2, 4, 6 with scheduled visits 0.933, 0.953, 0.943; with irregular visits 0.933, 0.957, 0.920; beta 0.953 (pseudo) and 0.947 (likelihood); all within tolerance (0.048), the irregular t = 6 cell the lowest. | done |
| A constant or collinear covariate is aliased (`nan` coefficient, listed in `aliased`, one warning, predictions taking it as 0), as the other regressions. | principle | Principle 12. | done |
| No AIC/BIC or `maximum` attribute: both models are registered as fits that do not maximise a likelihood. | proposed | The objective is a working (pseudo- or Poisson) likelihood over a step-function baseline whose dimension grows with the data. | done |
| The parametric intensity models (`CrowAMSAA`, `Duane`, `CoxLewis`, `HPP`, `ProportionalIntensityNHPP`, ...) take panel counts as interval-counted rows: `c=2`, `x = [previous, this]`, `n` the count. `handle_xicn` now accepts `n = 0` on a counted row (`c=2` or `c=-1`); 0 is still refused on an exact-event or end-of-observation row, and data whose every counted row is 0 have no events. | proposed | The grouped-count likelihood was already the Poisson likelihood of the increments; only the refusal of a zero count stood between it and panel data, and an inspection that found nothing is information. | done |
| The reference fixture is simulated (`panel_counts` in `make_fixtures.py`: 85 units at their own whole-month visits, a treatment indicator and two baseline covariates, gamma-frailty counts); the R values come from spef 1.0.9 (installed from the CRAN archive) run on it. spef's own example data (`blaTum`, the bladder tumour panel counts) are not copied into the repository; the check on them was run locally: `MPL` coefficients (treatment, size, num) = (-1.326383, -0.062618, 0.250410) and the baseline at every visit time agree to about 1e-8, the pseudo-likelihood MCF with spef's isotonic code to 1e-12, and the likelihood fit (-0.7892, -0.0283, 0.2566) is within 0.01 of `MLs` (-0.7923, -0.0299, 0.2576). | proposed | The repository's rule for data from other packages (as for `ebmt` and `cav`): a simulated fixture of the same character, and the package's own data checked locally and reported. Shipping the bladder data as a dataset is a separate decision for the maintainer. | done |
### Joint frailty model of recurrent events and a terminal event (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `JointFrailty` (fitter) and `JointFrailtyModel` in a new module `surpyval.recurrent.joint_frailty`, the fitter also at the top level. The recurrent intensity is `u r0(t) exp(beta'Z)` and the terminal hazard `u^alpha lambda0(t) exp(gamma'Z)`, one frailty `u` per item (Liu, Wolfe and Huang 2004; Rondeau et al. 2007; R `frailtypack::frailtyPenal` with `terminal()`). One covariate matrix `Z` enters both parts, each with its own coefficients. | proposed | The model of the brief, and frailtypack's. A separate covariate matrix for the terminal part (frailtypack's `formula.terminalEvent`) is not offered: a gap. | done |
| How the terminal event is supplied: the rows of every recurrent model (`c = 0` an event at its age, one `c = 1` row per item closing its observation) plus `terminal`, 1 on that closing row where the terminal event closed it (or a dict `{item: 0/1}`). `terminal = 1` on an event row is refused, saying to give both rows. Exactly one closing row per item is required; left- and interval-censored counts (`c = -1, 2`) and counts `n` are refused. `fit_from_df(df, x_col, i_col, c_col, terminal_col, Z_cols, tl_col)`. | proposed | It adds one flag to the package's recurrent data, as frailtypack's `terminal(death)` column does. An `EventHistoryData`/`ItemHistory` input (an absorbing state) is not read yet: a gap. | done |
| Weibull baselines for both parts, `R0(t) = (t / scale)^shape` (the power-law, Crow-AMSAA intensity), as frailtypack's `hazard = "Weibull"` (the same parameterisation, checked). No spline or piecewise baselines. | proposed | Parametric first, as the brief asks; the Weibull is the repairable-systems standard and frailtypack's parametric option. Splines are a gap. | gap (splines) |
| Both time scales: `time_scale="calendar"` (the item's age, Andersen-Gill; frailtypack `recurrentAG=TRUE`) and `"gap"` (time since the last event; `recurrentAG=FALSE`), the terminal hazard on the age in both. Delayed entry (`tl`) on the calendar scale only, the likelihood divided by the frailty-averaged chance of being free of the terminal event at entry; refused on the gap scale (the gap open at entry is unknown), as are two events of one item at the same age (a gap of 0). | proposed | Both are standard and cheap here. Conditioning on survival to entry is what a selected sample needs; recurrences before entry are unobserved and integrate out. | done |
| Gamma (mean 1, variance `theta`) and log-normal (`log u ~ N(0, theta)`, frailtypack's `RandDist="LogN"` and the existing `WeibullFrailty`'s) frailty. | proposed | The two families every reference offers; the same `theta` meanings as the shared-frailty models. | done |
| The frailty integral `int u^D exp(-A u - B u^alpha) dG(u)` is computed by a trapezoid rule in `w = log u` over each item's own range (from the integrand's mode, by Newton's method on the concave log-integrand, out to where it has fallen by `e^-36`), with steps of at most `0.3 / max(1, |alpha|)` and a quarter of the integrand's scale at its mode; the nodes are held when autograd differentiates. Not Gauss-Hermite (adaptive or not), not Gauss-Laguerre. | proposed | Adaptive Gauss-Hermite (the joint longitudinal models', and the log-normal frailty's in `families.py`) was tried first: the gamma frailty's integrand has an exponential left tail in `w` that it follows badly (log-integral 4e-4 out at `theta = 2` with no events). The trapezoid rule on an analytic integrand that has died away converges geometrically; it agrees with scipy's adaptive quadrature to 1e-10 for `theta` from 0.05 to 5 and with the gamma closed form (`alpha = 1`) to 1e-11 (tests). frailtypack integrates the gamma frailty with a fixed Gauss-Laguerre rule (32 nodes by default), a different rule, so gamma-frailty estimates of the two are compared by log-likelihood at each other's estimates (see the reference row). | done |
| The fit: the model without frailty (both parts separately) first, then the full model from it with `theta = 0.5` and `alpha` 1 and 0 (and from `init`, if given), BFGS on autograd's gradient in the logs of the positive parameters and the coefficients of standardised columns, then Newton steps on autograd's Hessian; the best kept, `maximum = "verified"` only for a positive-definite Hessian and a Newton decrement below 1e-6 nats. The covariance is the inverse of autograd's Hessian in the natural parameters. | proposed | Standardised columns keep a covariate's units from mattering (principle 6, tested); exact derivatives of the quadrature make the verification and the covariance exact. | done |
| `theta` at 0 (no frailty) is a boundary: where the full fit is no better than the model without frailty, or `theta < 1e-6`, the model is reported as that one, `theta = 0` and `alpha = nan` (not identified), with one warning; both are held out of the covariance. | proposed | Without a frailty `alpha` has no meaning; reporting the boundary avoids a silent search drifting to `log theta = -inf`. | done |
| `frailty_tests()`: the likelihood-ratio test of `theta = 0` against the model without frailty (the two parts fitted separately), p-value from the 50:50 mixture of chi-square(1) and chi-square(2); and of `alpha = 0` (refit with `alpha` held at 0) against chi-square(1). | proposed | Near `theta = 0` the frailty acts through two variances on the edge of their range, `theta` (recurrent) and `alpha^2 theta` (terminal), and `alpha` is not identified under the null (Davies 1987). The first reference tried, the shared-frailty models' 50:50 mixture of chi-square(0) and chi-square(1), rejected a true null 18.0% of the time at 5% (300 replicates, the study below; tolerance 4.8 points): it is wrong here. The mixture of chi-square(1) and chi-square(2) (a variance on its edge tested with a second parameter; Self and Liang 1987, Stram and Lee 1994) was then chosen on that argument and the same study, unchanged (seed, size, tolerance), rerun: 5.0%. Because it was chosen after seeing that failure, a second size study was written down and committed before it was run once (new seed 24131; 1000 replicates of 120 units; a decreasing recurrent intensity, shape 0.8, and a steep terminal hazard, shape 2.5, so the terminal event closes most histories; a continuous covariate): 4.6% (tolerance 3.1 points), which passes. Still an approximation (Davies 1987); a parametric-bootstrap p-value would not rest on it (a gap). frailtypack reports a Wald test of `alpha`. | done |
| Bounds: Wald `param_cb` (log scale for the positive parameters) and the profile-likelihood `param_cb(method="lr")` (the shared `LikelihoodRegion`); delta-method `cif_cb`, `iif_cb`, `mcf_cb` (log scale) and `sf_cb` (log cumulative hazard). No bootstrap bounds. | proposed | The package's standard set for a parametric likelihood model; a bootstrap of a joint model is many refits of a several-second fit. | gap (bootstrap) |
| Predictions are for the population of items with covariates `Z` (`frailty=None`, averaged over the frailty) or for one item of given frailty (`frailty=u`). `cif`/`iif`: the recurrent intensity with no terminal event to stop it (`E[u] R0 exp(beta'Z)`); `mcf`: the expected count by `x` with the terminal event stopping it, `exp(beta'Z) int r0(s) E[u S_T(s | u)] ds` (calendar scale only; refused on the gap scale, where it needs simulation); the terminal event's `sf`, `ff`, `hf`, `Hf`, `df`. `frailties`: each item's posterior mean frailty (empirical Bayes, frailtypack's `frailty.pred`). | proposed | Both the conditional and the marginal reading are asked for in practice; `mcf` is the quantity a maintenance or care plan counts. | done |
| `random(size, Z, end=, random_state=)` (Z second, as in every model's `random`) simulates items (frailty, terminal age, recurrent events up to the earlier of the two) and returns the rows `fit` takes. | proposed | Calibration and users' what-ifs need it. | done |
| Reference: frailtypack 3.8.2 on a seeded synthetic fixture (`joint_frailty`, 250 items) rather than its `readmission` data, which is not copied into the repository (frailtypack's data). Log-normal, calendar and gap: every parameter within 0.2% and the log-likelihood within 2e-3. Gamma, calendar: the two integrate the frailty differently (a fixed Gauss-Laguerre rule there, a rule checked against scipy to 1e-10 here), so the estimates agree to a few per cent (parameters within 3%, coefficients within 0.02) and are compared by log-likelihood at each other's estimates: SurPyval's log-likelihood at frailtypack's estimate is below its own maximum by under 0.05. Gamma, gap: frailtypack returned no fit for this model on the fixture (istop 4), so there is no stored entry. On `readmission` (sex, Dukes stage, chemotherapy; run locally, not in the test suite, recorded in the R script): calendar gamma, frailtypack logLik -4227.03 with `theta` 1.04, `alpha` 1.03; SurPyval -4225.73 with `theta` 1.28, `alpha` 0.96, the coefficients within 0.05; SurPyval's log-likelihood at frailtypack's estimate is -4226.76. Calendar log-normal: frailtypack -4218.881, `sigma2` 1.3125; SurPyval -4218.869, 1.3121. Gap gamma: frailtypack -4159.13 (`theta` 0.741, `alpha` 1.531), SurPyval -4159.73 (0.703, 1.577), and -4159.78 at frailtypack's estimate. | proposed | With the log-normal frailty both use rules that agree closely; with the gamma frailty the rules differ, so the comparison is of log-likelihoods at each other's estimates. | done |
| Not built (each a gap): spline/penalised baselines, covariates that change over time, a frailty shared by a group of items (nested frailty), several terminal events (competing), several recurrent event types, interval-censored recurrences, weights, separate covariates for the terminal part, an `EventHistoryData` input. | proposed | Scope of a first version. | gap |
| Calibration (`calibration/test_coverage_joint_frailty.py`, fixed before the run: 300 replicates of 200 items, gamma `theta = 0.5`, `alpha = 1`, Weibull (10, 1.2) and (20, 1.5), `beta = 0.5`, `gamma = 0.3`, follow-up uniform (10, 20), seed 24117): standardised bias of every parameter within 0.37 of 0 (largest: terminal shape 0.36 and `alpha` 0.34, inside the 0.37 tolerance), reported standard error over spread 0.93 to 1.07; Wald coverage of the eight parameters 0.920 to 0.967 (recurrent shape 0.920 the lowest); profile-likelihood coverage `theta` 0.940, `alpha` 0.927; `sf_cb` at 10 0.943, `mcf_cb` at 10 0.980; power of the `alpha = 0` test 0.927; size of the `theta = 0` test 0.050 (after the change above; 0.180 before), and 0.046 in the second, pre-registered size study (1000 replicates, a different design). | proposed | Every check passes. The `mcf_cb` band is a little wide (0.98) and the `alpha` profile interval and the recurrent shape's Wald interval a little short; the biases of the terminal shape and `alpha` are near the edge of the tolerance, as a maximum-likelihood estimate of a power from 0.4 terminal events per item can be. | done |
### Multi-state models: semi-Markov ("clock reset") parametric models and the expected length of stay (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `clock="reset"` in `ParametricMultiState` and `ParametricMultiStateRegression` (`fit` and `fit_from_df`): each transition's hazard is a function of the time spent in its 'from' state, fitted on the durations `x - tl` (`x` where `tl` is missing), with no truncation. `tl` is read as the time the unit entered the state. A fitter whose `support` is not `(0, inf)` is refused (Normal, Gumbel, Beta, ...). | proposed | On this clock the likelihood still factorises over transitions, with each spell's duration as an ordinary (right-censored) time, so the existing fitters are reused unchanged; it is flexsurv's `Surv(Tstop - Tstart, status)` fit. A duration starts at 0 and has no upper limit. A spell whose entry into the state was not observed (a late entry into the study) cannot be told apart in spell data, so the meaning of `tl` is stated in the docs. | done |
| Predictions on the clock reset solve the renewal (Volterra) equations by numerical convolution, not by simulation: the entries into each state per cell of a uniform grid (taken as even over the cell), weights from cell integrals of each transition's density and each state's survival by 8-point Gauss-Legendre (on 30 cells halving towards 0 in the first cell, so a hazard infinite at 0 is integrated accurately). Without a cycle, one FFT convolution per transition in a topological order; with a cycle (recovery), the entries are built a move at a time (one convolution per transition per move) until the chance of a further move is below 1e-14 (at most 10 000 moves, with a warning). | proposed | Deterministic, so a prediction has no seed or Monte Carlo error, and differentiable in the parameters on a fixed grid, so the delta method applies as on the forward clock. The same scheme handles cyclic structures, so simulation is not needed for "general" structures. flexsurv's `pmatrix.simfs` is a simulation (its answer moves by about `sqrt(p(1-p)/M)`); `mstate` offers only `mssample`. No public path simulator was added. | done |
| The step is halved from 1/64 of the longest stay in the data until the occupation probabilities at the nodes over that span, from every state that can be left, change by at most 1e-7 (at most 2^17 cells, with a warning); the accuracy stated is about 1e-6 in a probability and 1e-6 of the time in a length of stay. A time between nodes uses the weights integrated to that time (no interpolation); a time within the first 64 cells gets a grid of its own of 64 cells ending at it. Between nodes the probabilities are scaled to add up to 1 (they miss it by under 1e-6 before), and the lengths of stay to `x - s`. The grid depends only on the model (and the covariate row), and its reach doubles past the span, so a prediction does not depend on the other times asked for. | proposed | The scheme is second order for smooth hazards, but with a shape below 1 the entries just after the start are far from even over a cell and it converges as about `h^1.3` to `h^1.8` there; at a tolerance of 1e-6 a closed-form check missed 1e-6 by up to 2x in the length of stay and near the start (1.3e-5 at 3.5 cells off a node). The tighter tolerance and the start's own grid bring every checked case inside 1e-6. | done |
| A prediction on the clock reset takes each unit to have just entered its state at `s`, so `P(s, t)` depends only on `t - s`. With no `from_state`, the default start (the earliest entry, where every unit at risk has just entered) uses the units' states in proportion, as on the forward clock; any other `s` without `from_state` is refused with the reason. | proposed | Units under observation at a later time have been in their states for different times, which the starting distribution does not record. | done |
| Bounds on the clock reset: the delta method over every transition's parameters through the convolution (central differences on the fitted model's grid), log(-log) scale by default, as on the forward clock. No simulation-of-parameters bounds (flexsurv's), bootstrap or likelihood-ratio bounds; no bounds on `transition_probabilities`. | proposed | One method for both clocks; its standard errors match the spread over 300 parameter draws to 15% (tested). Calibration (`calibration/test_coverage_semimarkov.py`, illness-death with Weibull hazards of the time in the state, one shape below 1; 400 replicates, n = 300, nominal 95%): P(ill) at 3 and 10 0.958 and 0.953, P(dead) at 10 0.960, time ill by 10 0.958, time healthy by 10 0.958 (`length_of_stay_cb`), all within tolerance, a little conservative. | done |
| `length_of_stay(x, state, s, from_state)` (and `length_of_stay_se`, `length_of_stay_cb`) for both clocks: the integral of the occupation probability from `s` to `x`, flexsurv's `totlos`. Forward clock: Simpson's rule on each interval of the forward equations' grid (the middle reached by the first half of the Magnus step); clock reset: from the same convolution, with the cell integrals of the survival integrated once more. Bounds: Wald on the logit of the fraction `L / (x - s)`, as `rmst_cb`. | proposed | The integral needs no grid of its own on either clock. The logit keeps the bounds inside `(0, x - s)`, the convention already used for the restricted mean survival time. Agrees with `totlos.fs` to 9e-9 of the time (forward, flexsurv's parameters). | done |
| The clock reset is not offered for `MultiStateProportionalHazards` or `MultiState` (they have no `clock` argument). | proposed | A Cox model per transition on durations is a sound fit (mstate supports it), but its probabilities need the convolution over step baseline hazards, which mstate itself only simulates (`mssample`); a fit without its predictions would be half a model. The Aalen-Johansen estimate is Markov by construction (its occupation probabilities are still consistent for non-Markov data, Datta and Satten 2001). To fit a Cox model on the durations, use `CoxPH` per transition on `x - tl`. | gap |
| The checks against R use `flexsurv` 2.3.2 on the MGUS spells and the simulated fixture (with recovery): the clock-reset fits by log-likelihood (Weibull, log-normal, exponential, Weibull PH on sex; within 6e-11) and parameters; the probabilities and lengths of stay with flexsurv's parameters against `pmatrix.simfs` / `totlos.simfs` (10^6 paths, seeded) within five Monte Carlo standard errors (largest 2.8); the forward lengths of stay against `totlos.fs` (deSolve, 1e-12 tolerances). The exact checks are a closed form (illness-death, Weibull hazards of the time in the state, one infinite at 0) and exponential hazards (where the clock reset is the Markov model) with recovery, both to 1e-6. | proposed | flexsurv has no exact clock-reset method, so its numbers can only bound SurPyval's to the simulation's error; the closed form and the Markov special case check the convolution itself. | done |
| Covariates that change during a stay are not taken (one row per spell, as on the forward clock). | proposed | The fitters take one covariate row per spell; a time-varying path within a stay would need the convolution with hazards depending on calendar time too. | gap |
### Mixed-effects Cox regression and the log-normal Cox frailty (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Two public shapes: `CoxFrailty.fit(..., family="lognormal")` for one grouping factor (it returns a `CoxFrailtyModel`, marginal predictions by default, as the gamma family), and a new `CoxMixedEffects` (`CoxMixedEffectsFitter`, `CoxMixedEffectsModel`) for any number of terms, with `RandomEffect` to name a term. `family` is a `fit` argument for the Cox frailty (the parametric `Frailty(dist, family=)` takes it at construction, as its fitters are per distribution). | proposed | One factor is a frailty model and belongs with `CoxFrailty`; several terms and random slopes need their own model (prediction needs a group per term and the slope covariates). The name spells out what `coxme` abbreviates, as `CoxFrailty` does. | done |
| Maximum likelihood as `coxme`: for given variances, the coefficients and random effects maximise the penalised partial likelihood (Newton, `CoxPH`'s partial likelihood on `[Z, random design]`); the variances maximise its Laplace approximation to the integrated partial likelihood with the coefficients at the joint maximum (L-BFGS-B, central-difference gradient, a second start near 0 and a snap to exactly 0 at the boundary). Random effects are parameterised as `b = L a` with `L` the Cholesky factor (as `lme4`), so a variance of exactly 0 is allowed; a term's covariance by standard deviations and canonical partial correlations. Not the REML-type choice of `coxph(frailty(dist = "gaussian"))`. | proposed | It is `coxme`'s method, so the results compare with it; the integrated likelihood is what `coxme` reports and is comparable across models. `coxph`'s gaussian frailty penalises the same likelihood but chooses the variance by a different rule (an approximate REML rather than maximum likelihood); at a fixed variance the two agree (tested). | done |
| Terms without a formula: `groups=` (a random intercept) or `random_effects=` a list of `RandomEffect(groups, slopes=, intercept=, correlated=, name=)`; a nested factor is a term for the outer factor and one for the pair (`RandomEffect((hospital, ward))`). In `fit_from_df`, `random_effects=` takes the bar notation; `(x | g)` without an explicit `1` or `0` is refused. Slopes are columns of the fixed `Z` (by position or name). | proposed | `lme4` reads `(x | g)` as intercept and slope, `coxme` as the slope alone; refusing it avoids a silent difference. Taking slopes from `Z` lets predictions read them from the same rows; a random slope without a fixed slope is unusual and not offered. | done |
| Fixed coefficients: Wald intervals from the inverse of the penalised information (its fixed block, `coxme`'s `vcov`). Variance components: no standard errors (as `coxme`), profile-likelihood intervals in `variance_cb()` (the other variance parameters re-maximised at each point; 0 is the lower bound where the profile there is within the cut-off; the correlations of a term with three or more effects get no bounds). The Cox frailty's log-normal `theta` keeps the Cox frailty's convention: a profile-curvature standard error and a log-scale Wald interval. | proposed | Calibration (400 replicates, 40 groups of 5, variance 0.49): Wald `beta` 0.9525, profile variance 0.950, the frailty's Wald `theta` 0.9775 (conservative). | done |
| `lr_test()` tests all random effects against `CoxPH` on the same rows: one variance, the 50:50 mixture of chi-square(0) and (1); `m` independent variances, the binomial chi-bar-square mixture; with correlations, the plain chi-square on the number of variance parameters (conservative), which the result says. | proposed | Self and Liang (1987). Size at 5% (1000 replicates, no group effect): 0.034, inside the tolerance and below 5%, as the Laplace approximation with 40 groups makes it. | done |
| Predictions are marginal by default (`marginal=True`), as `CoxFrailty`'s: the random effects of every term not named in `group=` are integrated out (the row's random part is normal, so this is a log-normal Laplace transform of its variance, available for every structure built). `group=` conditions on an observed group's predicted random effects; `marginal=False` sets the remaining ones to 0, the curve `coxme` predicts. | proposed | One default across the Cox frailty models; the marginal curve answers the common question about a unit from a group not yet seen, and the coxme curve is one argument away. | done |
| Refused, with what to use: `random()` (step baseline; a parametric frailty), `diagnose()` (`lr_test`, `variance_cb`, and `CoxPH.fit(...).diagnose()`), weights, interval and left censoring (`Frailty(Weibull, family="lognormal")`), `exact`/`kp` ties. Not built: sparse matrices for many groups (the design is dense), `coxme`'s user-defined variance structures (`varlist`, pedigrees), REML. | proposed | Each is a separate piece of work; the dense design is fine to a few hundred groups (37 groups with a random slope and 2,300 rows: about 5 s). | gap |
| The reference runs `coxme` 2.2.22's outer search with `reltol = 1e-14`. At its default tolerance the two optimisers stop at slightly different points (up to 0.014 in log-likelihood on a correlated-slope example run locally), so the fits are compared by log-likelihood (SurPyval's at least R's) and at R's variances, where every value R reports is reproduced to 1e-6. | proposed | Separates the likelihood from the optimiser. | done |
### Stratified and clustered Fine-Gray regression (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FineGray.fit(..., strata=None, cluster=None, strata_censoring="within")`; predictions `cif(x, Z, stratum=...)` and `sf(..., stratum=...)`; `fit_from_df(..., strata_col=, cluster_col=)`. A stratified model refuses a prediction without `stratum`, a model without strata refuses one with it. Rows with a missing stratum or cluster label are dropped with a warning. | proposed | The names `CoxPH` uses (`strata`, `stratum`, `strata_col`; `cluster` as in `CoxPH`'s `robust_covariance(cluster)`), and its handling of missing labels. | done |
| Strata: each stratum has its own subdistribution risk sets, Breslow baseline and (by default) its own censoring Kaplan-Meier `G`; the partial likelihood, information and the sandwich's meat are sums over strata. That is R `crrSC::crrs(ctype = 1)` (Zhou, Latouche, Rocha and Fine, 2011). `strata_censoring="common"` uses one `G` for all the strata (`ctype = 2`), its censoring term built from every stratum's events. | proposed | The paper's two settings (a few large strata, many small ones). On the seeded fixtures (`make_fixtures.fine_gray_strata`, `fine_gray_cluster`) it matches `crrs` (ctype 1 and 2, three strata and 100 small strata) to 4e-12 in coefficients and standard errors, and each stratum's CIF matches `crr` on the stratum's rows with the coefficients held, to 4e-15 (tests at 1e-7 and 1e-8). Run locally on crrSC's own example data (bce, center), it agrees to 1e-7. Calibration (three strata with their own baselines and censoring, n = 300): 95% coverage 0.945 and 0.940. | done |
| `crrs(ctype = 2)`'s variance sums the score residuals within each stratum (its C code clusters by stratum). SurPyval does not cluster by stratum unless asked: `strata_censoring="common", cluster=<strata>` reproduces `crrs(ctype = 2)` exactly. | proposed | Clustering by stratum is a choice about dependence, not about the censoring distribution; with three strata it gives a three-cluster sandwich (on the fixture its standard errors are 1.5 to 1.7 times the unit-level ones). With `ctype = 2` the strata must be coded 1, 2, ... for `crrs` to return a variance, so the reference script recodes them with `as.integer(factor(.))`. | done |
| Clusters: the estimate is the independence working model's (the fit without clusters), and the robust covariance sums each cluster's influence (the score residual and the censoring term, Fine and Gray's `eta + psi`) before squaring: R `crrSC::crrc` (Zhou, Fine, Latouche and Labopin, 2012). The model-based covariance (`robust=False`) ignores the clusters. A count `n` is that many units in the row's cluster. | proposed | Matches `crrc` to 3e-15 on the seeded fixture (`fine_gray_cluster`, tests at 1e-7), and to 1e-7 when run locally on crrSC's cdata; one-row clusters give exactly the unclustered covariance. Calibration (150 clusters of two sharing a covariate): 95% coverage 0.946 and 0.951 (0.889 and 0.948 ignoring the clusters). | done |
| The diagnostics (`diagnose()`, the proportional subdistribution hazards test, the added-covariate test, the martingale residuals) and `plot_partial_effects` (for strata) refuse a stratified or clustered fit, saying what to use instead. | proposed | Their tests take one baseline and independent units; stratified and cluster-robust forms are not built. | gap |
| A stratified model's `to_dict` stores each stratum's baseline under `"strata"` and no top-level baseline, and is stamped schema 4 (as the stratified Cox model). | proposed | A reader that does not know the strata then cannot take one stratum's baseline for the model's. | done |
| `CompetingRisksProportionalHazards(model="Fine-Gray")` does not take strata or clusters yet. | proposed | Kept to the single-cause `FineGray`; the per-cause wrapper can pass them through later. | gap |
| The reference fixtures are simulated (`make_fixtures.fine_gray_strata`: three strata with their own baselines and censoring; `fine_gray_cluster`: pairs sharing a covariate, dependent through a Gaussian copula), not crrSC's example data (bce, center, cdata). | proposed | As for `ebmt` and `cav`: data distributed inside a GPL R package with an unclear licence are not copied into the repository. The checks on crrSC's own data were run locally and agree to 1e-7. | done |

### Restricted mean time lost to a cause (13 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
