## 2026-10-15

### Working method

| Decision | By |
|---|---|
| One issue at a time. Explain it with a minimal example, then the maintainer decides, then build. | maintainer |
| Before merging, show before/after results on the real code, and list anything done beyond what was agreed. | maintainer |
| A long plan is a map, not a commitment; re-plan after each issue. | maintainer |

### AFT on baselines defined below 0 (Normal, Gumbel, Logistic)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keep these AFT models. AFT means S(t \| X) = S0(φ(X)·t), which rescales time about 0. No warning and no deprecation. | maintainer | By that definition everything follows: dependence on where 0 is, the sign flip before 0, the clock anchored at 0. | done |
| AFT and ALT are distinct. A model whose covariates shift the location is an accelerated life model, e.g. `AcceleratedLife(Normal, life_models.Linear)`. R's gaussian `survreg` "AFT" is that model. | maintainer | SurPyval's naming follows the textbook definition, not R's. | done (guide note) |
| A `GeneralLinear` life model (μ = a + b'X). | maintainer | So the location model can take several covariates. | #963 open |
| A time before 0 needs the covariate from that time to 0. A faster-ageing unit looks safer before 0: "it is what it is". | maintainer | It follows from the anchoring at 0. | done |

### Covariates must cover every time the answer depends on (#930, #952, #957)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Refuse a schedule or path that starts after the time origin, rather than holding its first value back. This is a breaking change. | maintainer | "This is a silent error." | merged (#951) |
| On baselines defined below 0, PH and PO need the covariates from −∞. AFT and AH are anchored at 0. In `fit_tvc`, a first `xl = 0` means the unit entered at 0, and `-inf` means followed since −∞. | maintainer | The model's maths depends on the whole stretch. | merged (#956) |
| Integrate a `CovariatePath` below 0, and from −∞ for PH and PO. | maintainer | — | merged (#958) |

### Weights stage 2: Aalen's additive model

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AalenAdditive.fit(w=)` is timereg's weighted estimator: each row counts `n w` in both `X'WYX` and `X'W dN`, and the variance of `B(t)` is always the robust one, `sum_i n_i psi_i psi_i'` with `psi_i` the unit's martingale residuals mapped through `(X'WYX + P)^-1` (`timereg::aalen(..., weights = w, robust = 1)`, `robvar.cum`). The unweighted fit keeps the optional variation. | proposed | The decided `w` semantics; timereg is the same estimator, matched to 1e-15 on two data sets. | done |
| With `penalizer`, the weighted penalty is `penalizer * sum_i n_i w_i (Z_i - mean_w)^2 / (sum_i n_i - 1)`: it scales with the weights as `X'WYX` does, so the fit is scale-free and is the unweighted one at `w = 1`. Whole-number weights then differ from counts (the counted penalty divides by `sum n w - 1`); without a penalty they are equal to the last digit. | proposed | Scale invariance and weights-as-counts cannot both hold for a penalty that does not grow with the data; scale invariance is the rule for `w`. | done |
| `constant_effects_test()` refuses a weighted fit (`WeightedRefusal`), pointing to the robust bands of `plot()` / `cumulative_coefficients_cb()`. | proposed | Its simulated null is built and size-checked for unweighted data only; a weighted multiplier version would need its own size study. | done (gap) |
| `concordance()` of a weighted Aalen fit scores the rows unweighted, as for weighted Cox and parametric fits. | proposed | Consistency with the other weighted models; a weighted C is a separate change. | done |
| Design-based check by simulation (`scripts/studies/aalen_weighted_coverage.py`, 500 replicates, fixed seeds 20261105+r, informative sampling of censored units): the 95% robust bounds on `B_0(5)` and `B_z(5)` cover 0.936 and 0.948 (MC se 0.011, 0.010); the classical variance with the weights read as counts covers 0.850 and 0.884; the unweighted fit 0.000 and 0.674. | proposed | Evidence that the robust variance is the valid one, and why the classical one is not offered. | done |

### 0.27 WP2a: naming the rows of a parametric covariance (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Parametric.covariance_names` names the rows of `covariance()`: the distribution's parameters, then `lfp_p` (lfp fits), then `f0` (zi fits). A property derived from the model's options; nothing new is saved, so `to_dict` and schema 4 are unchanged. | proposed | The same name and convention as the degradation process models and mixtures; the order is the one the fitter already builds. | done |
| A parameter fixed at fit time keeps its name and a zero row and column. | proposed | That is what `covariance()` already returns; names and rows stay one to one. | done |
| An offset `gamma` stays out of `covariance()` (held at its estimate; the other rows are conditional on it). | proposed | `scripts/studies/offset_gamma_wald.py` (1000 replicates, n = 200, seed 20261105): the 95% Wald interval for gamma from the numerical Hessian covered 0.889 at shape 3.5, 0.873 at 2.5, 0.762 at 1.5 (MC se 0.007), and the Hessian was unusable in 1000/1000 fits at 0.8. `param_cb("gamma")` already refuses Wald and offers likelihood-ratio and bootstrap bounds; adding a row would also change the saved covariance's shape. | done |
| `parameter_names` stays the distribution's names (the entries of `params`); it is not made equal to `covariance_names`. | proposed | `parameter_names` labels `params`, and dozens of callers (bootstrap, Bayes, regression, recurrent, copulas, `with_params`, `to_dict`'s `param_names`) zip it with `params`; `covariance_names` starts with it. | done |
| `hess_inv` stays the distribution's block (no `lfp_p` or `f0`); its docs now say so and point to `covariance()` with `covariance_names`. | proposed | Changing its shape would change saved models; `covariance()` is the variance API. | done |

### 0.27 WP2c: parametric competing-risks lifetime (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ParametricCompetingRisks.mean()` integrates `S(t)` by `scipy.integrate.quad` on a log-time axis (`t = s e^y`, `s` the all-cause median), split at `y = 0`, to `epsrel = 1e-12`; a survival falling no faster than `1/t` at `t = 1e12 s` to `1e14 s` gives `inf`; a support below 0 subtracts `int_{-inf}^0 F`. | proposed | The integrand on the log axis is smooth over any scale; exact closed forms (exponential, common-shape Weibull, LogLogistic pair) match to 1e-10. | done |
| With a cure ceiling `mean()` is `inf` and `mean(defective=True)` is `int t dF = int (S - S(inf)) dt`. | proposed | The semantics and wording of `Parametric.mean`. | done |
| `qf(p)` solves `H(t) = -log(1 - p)` everywhere (not `ff(t) = p` below the median), by Brent's method on `log t`, bracketed by the causes' quantiles at `1 - (1 - p)^(1/K)` and `p`. | proposed | `F = 1 - exp(-H)`, so it is the same equation; on the hazard scale small and large `p` both keep their digits, so one residual does both halves. | done |
| `sf`, `ff`, `df` and `qf` take `event=k`, giving cause `k`'s net function (its own model). `mean` takes no `event`: the net mean is `model.models[k].mean()`. | proposed | The nonparametric `CompetingRisks.sf/ff(x, event)` are net; `hf`/`Hf(x, event)` are already cause-specific, so `df = hf * sf` holds per cause too. | done |
| Covariance, `with_params` and confidence bounds on these functions are left to a later piece. | proposed | Scope of this piece. | open |

### 0.27 WP2f: custom distribution recipes in flexsurv's parameterisations (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The generalised gamma and generalised F recipes are documented in Prentice's parameterisations, as `flexsurv` (`gengamma`: mu, sigma, Q; `genf`: mu, sigma, Q, P), beside the existing Stacy / Kalbfleisch-Prentice recipes, which stay. | proposed | Prentice's form holds the lognormal at a finite Q, so one fit tests the Weibull, lognormal and gamma by likelihood ratio, and results compare one to one with `flexsurvreg`. Documentation only: no distribution is added to the package. | proposed |
| The generalised gamma recipe takes the lognormal branch at exactly `Q == 0` (a Python `if`), so `fixed={'Q': 0}` gives the lognormal exactly; nothing is done near 0, where the gamma branches already agree with the lognormal to O(Q). | proposed | The same rule as flexsurv. A smooth switch near 0 would add code users copy for no gain in the fits checked. | proposed |

### 0.27 WP2d: fast residual-life draws past the quantile limit (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Past a cumulative hazard of 20, `conditional_gaps` inverts `H` by Newton's method with the hazard rate, stepping on `log H` against `log x` where all are positive (exact in one step for a Weibull) and on `H` against `x` otherwise, inside a bracket; a step leaving the bracket bisects it (or doubles the distance from `v` while nothing above the root is known). | proposed | 2.8 to 3.4 evaluations a draw where bracketing took 12 to 15; the log scale makes the step nearly exact in the upper tail of most lifetimes. | done |
| A draw stops when a step is below 1e-9 of the point, or below 1e-4 with the last two steps predicting an error after it under machine epsilon (`step^3 / previous^2`), or when the bracket closes to rounding. | proposed | Newton's error squares each step, so this stops one evaluation earlier without losing accuracy: agreement with the old solver and with mpmath roots to 1e-13. | done |
| The hazard rate is the lifetime's `hf`; without one, `exp(log f + H)` from `log_df` (or `log df`). A discrete or non-parametric lifetime (whose `hf` is the jumps of a step function), an undefined or infinite `H` (past a bounded support) or 30 steps without converging hand the draw to the old bracketing solver. | proposed | Newton only where `h` is a true derivative; the old solver stays as the safe path. | done |

### 0.27 WP4g: the published deprecation policy and the public-name snapshot (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The policy is its own page, `docs/Deprecation Policy.rst`, beside the changelog in the SurPyval section of the menu, with a short "Stability and deprecation" section on the docs home linking to it; the contributor steps are in `docs/Contributing.rst` ("Deprecating and removing a name"). | proposed | Users read the home page and the changelog; contributors read Contributing. One page holds the rules so the two do not drift. | done |
| "Public" for the policy and the snapshot: `surpyval.__all__`, and the names of `surpyval.univariate.{parametric, nonparametric, regression, competing_risks}`, `recurrent`, `degradation`, `multivariate`, `ml`, `life_models`, `metrics`, `diagnostics`, `bayes`, `bayes.prior`, `test_planning`, `datasets`, `pymc` and `utils`. A namespace with an `__all__` promises that list; one without promises the names it defines with no leading underscore (not modules, not names imported from elsewhere). | proposed | The namespaces the documentation imports from, plus those `test_completeness.py` walks; `surpyval.utils` already says its `__all__` is its promise. | done |
| Not public, may change without a warning: names with a leading underscore, names of `surpyval.utils` outside its `__all__`, the `surpyval.beta` tier, and `surpyval.tests`. | proposed | What the `beta` and `utils` docstrings and `test_private_imports.py` already say, written down in one place. | done |
| The snapshot covers module-level names only; methods, attributes and arguments are public under the policy but not frozen by a test (their removals are already enforced by `test_removed_arguments.py` and the `REMOVED_IN` scan). Deeper module paths (`surpyval.univariate.parametric.weibull`) are not watched. | proposed | A frozen list of every method and argument of every model would be thousands of entries and change with every feature; the namespaces are what users import. | open |
| A name gone from the public surface counts as properly retired when using it still works with a `DeprecationWarning` or `FutureWarning`, or raises an error whose message names a replacement ("removed in v", ": it is in ", "; it is now "). It stays in the list until its hint is retired; dropping it then (or removing a name with no deprecation) needs `--update --force`. | proposed | Checks the behaviour users see rather than a second list that must be kept in step with `removed_names.py`; covers the moved-name hints (`surpyval.TrendTestResult`) and removed modules (`surpyval.utils.score`) too. | done |
| The list is a text file, `surpyval/tests/public_surface.txt`, one dotted name a line, shipped as package data so the installed test suite runs it; regenerated only by `python -m surpyval.tests.test_public_surface --update`. | proposed | One line a name gives a readable diff in review; package data as for the reference fixtures. | done |
| Numbers may differ in their last digits between releases, platforms and numpy/scipy versions, within the optimiser's or integrator's tolerance, without a changelog line; a deliberate change to a result is listed under Changed or Fixed with its size where known. | proposed | What the changelog already does (e.g. the 0.26 accelerated-life fits "move by about 1e-5"); now stated to users. | open |
| Before 1.0, a change of behaviour that cannot carry a warning (a corrected result, a new default, a convention made consistent, as 0.27's coefficient signs) may come in any release, listed under Changed. The page also says releases come often, so one release of deprecation can be short, and suggests pinning. | proposed | Honest about the current practice; downstream code was caught out by short windows. | open |
| The page says "Version 1.0 will mark the interface as stable", and that anything the policy then promises beyond today's rules is set out before 1.0. No longer window or other post-1.0 promise is made yet. | proposed | The maintainer decides what 1.0 promises (for example a deprecation window of more than one release); the page commits to nothing beyond today's mechanism. | changed (2026-11-12): the 1.0 promise is set (semantic versioning; deprecations last at least two minor releases and six months), see "The interface at 1.0" |

### diagnose() of Aalen's additive hazards model (WP6 6f)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The `constant_effects` rows are the model's own `constant_effects_test()` (KS statistic and p in the row, CvM p in the detail, the whole table with the baseline in `d.tables`), not a second implementation. `n_boot` is its `n_sim` (default 1000, timereg's), `random_state` its seed. | proposed | One test, one number: the report and the method cannot disagree. | done |
| The `constant_effects` rows' verdicts are set: `ok` whatever the p-value (`warn` when the test cannot be computed, an aliased covariate). The advice reads the p-value: `AdditiveHazards.fit` when no checked effect changes (and no hazard is shown negative), how to read a changing effect otherwise. | proposed | Aalen's model allows effects that change, so a small p-value is not a fault of the model; a `fail` would make `d.ok` False for a correctly specified fit. | proposed |
| The p-values keep the model's event-multiplier null, not timereg's martingale-residual one, so they are not compared with timereg's. | proposed | timereg 2.0.7 rejected constant effects in 47% (n = 100) and 17% (n = 300) of simulated data sets at the 5% level (200 replicates each, seeds 4500 and 4700; `scripts/studies/aalen_timereg_size.R`); SurPyval's rejected 3-3.5% at n = 300. | done |
| The statistics are checked against timereg where both anchor the straight line at the same time; where SurPyval's estimate stops before the last event time (it anchors at the last time `B` is estimated, timereg at the last event time with `B` held) they are checked on timereg's grid. The KS supremum keeps the left limits of the steps (at least timereg's). | proposed | The model's documented definitions; changing them is a change to the model, not to the diagnostics. | done |
| `non_negative_hazard` is a test: the largest fall of any observed covariate row's estimate below its running maximum, in standard errors at the last event time, against the same drawdowns of the resampled error processes (the null of a zero hazard, least favourable, so conservative). | proposed | A first, pointwise screen (falls beyond 1.96 standard errors of the increment, warning above 5% of units) warned on 73.5% of null data sets and was replaced before commit. The test rejected 0/200 null data sets and 180/200 with a convex hazard. | done |
| Weighted fits are refused (`WeightedRefusal`, pointing to the robust bands of `plot()` / `cumulative_coefficients_cb()`). | proposed | Both nulls are simulated for unweighted data, as `constant_effects_test()` already refuses. | done (gap) |
| The family's `issue` is the public diagnose() design issue (867). | proposed | No public issue exists for this piece; the registry needs a number. | proposed |

### 0.27 WP6e: diagnose() for distributional regression (WP6 6e)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Three checks, the PH/AFT family's shape: `distribution` (Cox-Snell residuals on exponential paper plus the AIC screen), `functional_form` (likelihood-ratio test of a quadratic term) and `influence` (one-step dfbeta). No outliers or calibration check. | proposed | The three questions asked of a distributional model; the calibration check is built for the PH and AFT kinds only, and a deviance-residual outlier screen adds little beside influence here. | proposed |
| The report's covariates are the columns of `Z`; a family column `parameter` names the parameter a row is about, and `columns=` picks columns (all their parameters). | proposed | One covariate can be on several parameters; naming rows by `alpha_load` would make `columns=` take coefficient names, unlike every other family. | proposed |
| The Cox-Snell residuals get no test of their own (drawn only); the distribution is screened by AIC against the other prebuilt `<Name>Distributional` fitters, warning when one is more than 2 lower, as the PH/AFT families do. | proposed | Consistency with the PH/AFT families (estimated parameters make a KS-type test of the residuals invalid). | proposed |
| AIC candidates carry the model's covariates by role: the columns of the model's life parameter (scale, location or rate: `LIFE`) on the candidate's life parameter, the union of the model's other parameters' columns on each of the candidate's others; for a distribution with no life parameter listed (Beta) every column on every parameter. | proposed | Like for like for the common two-parameter case (the default fit puts every column on every parameter, which this reproduces). | proposed |
| Functional form tests each covariate only on the parameters whose predictor holds it; a covariate with two values is skipped (note). Whether a covariate left off a parameter belongs there is not checked; the note points to refitting and `likelihood_ratio_test(parameter=)`. | proposed | The quadratic test is about form; adding a term is the existing LR test's job. | proposed |
| The functional-form plot draws each unit's score residual `d log L_i / d eta_ik` against the covariate (lowess), not the martingale residual. | proposed | It is defined per parameter and for every row whatever its censoring or truncation; for a log scale it is `-beta` times the martingale residual (tested). | proposed |
| Weighted fits (`w`) are refused with a `WeightedRefusal` (the LR statistics are not chi-squared and the AIC not comparable), pointing to `diagnose()` of the unweighted fit; penalised fits are refused with the penalised-inference message, as the Cox family does. | proposed | No family supports weights; the PH family already fails on a weighted fit (via `aic()`), so the refusal here is explicit. | proposed |
| `Family.issue` is 867 (the public diagnose() design issue). | proposed | No public issue of its own. | proposed |

### 0.27 WP5b: delayed entry in the cause-specific Cox model (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CompetingRisksProportionalHazards.fit`, `fit_penalised` and `FineGray.fit` take `tl` as their last argument (after `w`), not after `n` as `CoxPH` does. | proposed | Inserting it after `n` would move `model`, `tie_method` and `center` for positional callers; `ParametricCompetingRisks.fit` also has `tl` after its options. | done |
| The cause-specific fit passes `tl` to each cause's `CoxPH.fit` (risk set `tl < t <= x`); no risk-set code of its own. A `tl` with no finite entry (a `SurpyvalData` without truncation) is dropped, so that fit is the unweighted one without `tl` to the last bit. | proposed | One risk-set implementation; bit-identity is the spec. | done |
| `FineGray`, `model="Fine-Gray"` and `gray_test` refuse `tl` only where some entry is at or after the first time; entries all before it are taken and change nothing. | proposed | Such entries leave every risk set as it is, and `fit(data)` passes a `tl` of `-inf` for data without truncation. | done |
| Fine-Gray and Gray's test with delayed entry (Geskus 2011 truncation weights) are not built; the refusal names `CompetingRisksProportionalHazards.fit(..., tl=)` and `CompetingRisks.fit(..., tl=)` (Aalen-Johansen, added in WP5a), and for Gray's test the cause-specific `logrank(..., tl=)`. | proposed | Scope of this piece; the cause-specific model and the Aalen-Johansen estimate cover the questions. | open (gap) |
| `FineGray.fit_from_df` gains `tl_col`, which reaches the same refusal. | proposed | Principle 14: a DataFrame entry point fills every data argument of `fit`. | done |
| The R reference rounds the `pbc` entries to 0.01 years. | proposed | Unrounded, `years * 0.2` equals other patients' `years` to within rounding, and `coxph`'s `timefix` merges such times while SurPyval compares them exactly, so the two disagreed by 7e-5 in a coefficient for a reason that is not the model's. The `competing` data's exact ties of entry and event times check the `tl < t` convention. | done |
| The cumulative incidences are checked against R's multi-state `survfit` under Breslow ties only (to 1e-10). Under Efron ties SurPyval's `cif` uses the Efron baseline increments and R's multi-state curve does not, so they differ by about 1e-2 on tied data, with or without `tl`. | proposed | The difference predates this piece; whether the Efron-tie `cif` should follow R is a separate question. | open |

### Delayed entry in the Aalen-Johansen estimate (WP5 5a)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CompetingRisks.fit` takes `tl` as its last argument (after `w`), and `fit_from_df` takes `tl_col` last (a column name or one number for every unit). | proposed | Inserting it before `how` would break positional calls; `ParametricCompetingRisks.fit` also has it after `how`. | done |
| The risk sets come from the existing `xcnt_to_xrd(x, c, n, t)` (the `(entry, exit]` rule of `KaplanMeier`); `tl` is checked by `xcnt_handler` (each `tl` strictly below its `x`). With no `tl` the call is the same as before, and the estimates and bounds are bit-identical to `dev` (checked on the conformance data and 300 tied rows, weighted and unweighted, both `how`s). | proposed | One truncation path for every non-parametric estimate. | done |
| The unweighted fit keeps Aalen's (cmprsk) variance with delayed entry: it is a function of the numbers at risk and of failures only, so it carries over (Andersen, Borgan, Gill and Keiding 1993, IV.4). No R function computes it with entry times (`cuminc` has none; `mstate`'s Aalen-type estimate is a different one), so it is checked against R's robust SE (within 6% on PBC by age, where the early risk sets are small), by the identities (a unit split where it re-enters changes nothing) and by `scripts/studies/aalen_johansen_entry_coverage.py` (1000 replicates, seeds 20261105+r): 95% coverage 0.962 and 0.952 at t = 2 and 6 (MC se 0.006, 0.007), bias 0.0012 and 0.0002 (ignoring entry: -0.074, -0.059). | proposed | Switching the unweighted variance to the robust one only when `tl` is given would make one model's bounds depend on whether an all-zero `tl` was passed. | done |
| A weighted fit with `tl` gives the robust (infinitesimal jackknife) variance with entry-aware risk indicators, matching `survfit(Surv(tl, x, factor(e)) ~ 1, weights = w, id = )$std.err` to 1e-10. | proposed | The same estimator as the weighted fit without entry. | done |
| The R reference uses `timefix = FALSE`. | proposed | `survfit` merges times that differ by round-off by default, and age + follow-up has such near-ties; SurPyval takes the numbers as given. | done |
| Right truncation stays refused (`fit(data)`'s message: "This fit takes no right truncation"). | proposed | The Aalen-Johansen risk sets need each unit followed to its exit; no competing-risks model here takes it. | done (gap) |
| `gray_test`, cause-specific Cox and Fine-Gray are untouched here (half 2). `pseudo_cif` keeps refusing `tl` with its own message (jackknife pseudo-values with delayed entry are a separate question); `plot` and `cb` work from the fitted risk sets, so they need no change. | proposed | Scope of this piece. | open |

### 0.27 WP1d: the left-truncated SBC scenario's generator (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The calibration study's left-truncated scenario draws each lifetime on the survival scale, `x = alpha (H(tl) - log(1 - U))^(1/beta)`, with the same uniforms (and so the same seeds) as the old inverse-cdf draw `qf(U(F(tl), 1))`; it equals that draw in exact arithmetic (every replicate but r = 299 agrees to 3e-11). | proposed | The old draw was `inf` when `F(tl)` rounded to 1 (r = 299: truth alpha 9.5, beta 3.6, entry 36.8), a fault of the simulation that refused one fit and hid the scenario's result. A generator bug, not a change of the study's rules: seeds, replicates and pass rules are as fixed before the first run. | done |
| The rerun's result is reported as found and kept as a strict expected failure: no fit refuses, ranks uniform (chi-square p 0.43, 0.55, 0.21 for alpha, beta, B10), 95% coverage 0.972, 0.962, 0.970 against 0.95 +- 0.02 (alpha and B10 fail). An investigation on 1,000 fresh replicates (seeds 9100 + r, 9600 + r) gave 0.958, 0.937, 0.933 with p 0.94, 0.43, 0.74, so it is taken as this seed set's excursion, as for "Weibull complete, informative". | proposed | Never loosen a rule or change a seed to make a study pass; record the numbers. | done |

### 0.27 WP1f: a second start for a stalled penalised fit (5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `solve_penalised` searches a second time, from the unpenalised fit (BFGS on the smooth part from the usual start), only when the first search fails its optimality check (`verified` false), and keeps the answer with the smaller penalised objective (the second also when equal to rounding and only it is verified). | proposed | A first answer that passes the check is a local minimum and is returned unchanged, so converged fits move by nothing and cost nothing. On the Rossi Weibull AFT lasso path (fin, age, prio) the stalled fit went from 1.58917 to 1.57922, all KKT conditions to 6e-16; the extra search took about 0.2 s, only on that fit. In the existing penalised tests 0 of 639 fits triggered it. | done |
| No warm start along `penalty_path`: each fit on the path still starts afresh. | proposed | `penalty_path` promises each fit is the one `fit_penalised` gives at that penalty; a warm start would make the path's fit depend on its neighbours. The second start fixes the stall in `fit_penalised` itself. | done |

### Parametric with_params and bootstrap refits (WP3 3a)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Parametric.with_params(params)` takes either the distribution's parameters (unchanged) or the full vector in `covariance_names` order (fixed parameters included); any other length or shape is a `ValueError` naming both lengths and the names. | proposed | The shared WP3 contract; a draw from `covariance()` maps straight back to a model, and existing callers keep working. | done |
| The offset is `with_params(params, gamma=...)`, kept from the fit when not given. A model without an offset refuses `gamma=` and points to `dist.from_params(params, gamma=..., **model.extras)`. | proposed | `gamma` is outside the covariance (WP2). `with_params` keeps the model's structure; `from_params` is where a structure is chosen, so adding an offset belongs there. | done |
| `lfp_p=` and `f0=` keywords are accepted with the short vector only (refused, with the reason, when the full vector already holds them, and when the model has no such parameter). | proposed | `MixtureModel.with_params(params, w=)` is the precedent: a keyword with the short form, refused with the full one. | done |
| A drawn `lfp_p` outside (0, 1], `f0` outside [0, 1), or `f0 >= lfp_p` is refused, not clipped; the message says to leave such draws out, draw on the logit scale, or use `bootstrap()`. | proposed | As `MixtureModel` refuses a weight below 0; clipping would pile draws on the edge silently and bias the spread. | done |
| `_estimates()` gives `params`, then `lfp_p` and `f0` where the model has them, as floats; `MixtureModel._estimates()` gives the components' parameters row by row, then the weights. | proposed | The `covariance_names` order of each; `with_params(model._estimates())` reproduces every prediction (exactly, in the conformance check over every registered case that has both). | done |
| `Parametric.bootstrap(n_boot=200, random_state=None)` returns a `ParametricBootstrap` built on the bounds' private refits: the same refits for the same `n_boot` and integer seed (cached on the model). Its `params` are each refit's own estimates, not moved onto an edge as the bounds' internal values are, so `with_params(params[i], gamma=gamma[i])` is refit `i`; failed refits are left out and counted; refits with no finite or an unverified maximum are kept and counted, as for the bounds. | proposed | One resampling scheme for bounds and refits; rows that rebuild their refit exactly. | done |
| Weighted fits are case-resampled, as the bootstrap bounds are; a weighted limited-failure or zero-inflated fit is refused, as its bootstrap bounds are, and the message points to draws from the robust covariance with `with_params`. Refusals of `bootstrap()` (no data, discrete, left- or interval-censored) say to draw from the covariance instead of "use method='wald'". | proposed | Follows the existing bounds; refuse only where they refuse. | done |
| `ParametricBootstrap.to_dict` holds the names, parameters, offsets and counts, not the refitted models; there is no `from_dict`. | proposed | The models are rebuilt from the rows with `with_params`; a result record, like `LikelihoodContour`. | done |

### diagnose() of fit_tvc parametric PH and AFT models (WP6 6g)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A unit's Cox-Snell residual (PH) is its entry `H(tl \| Z_first)` (none at `-inf`) plus the sum of its rows' increments `H(stop \| Z_row) - H(start \| Z_row)`; its martingale residual is its event indicator less the increments. An AFT unit's standardised residual is its accumulated age `psi`, truncated at its age at a finite entry on a baseline below 0. | proposed | The counting-process residuals of Therneau and Grambsch (2000, ch. 4), consistent with the likelihood each fit maximises; they equal the ordinary fit's for constant covariates (tested). Against flexsurv on the heart data to 1e-4 (observed 3e-6). | proposed |
| The likelihood-ratio tests (step at the median, quadratic term, shape per level) use the fit's own likelihood: the left-truncated rows for PH, the accumulated-age likelihood (rebuilt in `ph_aft._accumulated_neg_ll` with the covariates as the parameters are relative to) for AFT. The quadratic term is the square of each row's value. | proposed | Exact for the model; flexsurv's step and quadratic statistics agree to 1e-7. | proposed |
| `influence` and `outliers` are per unit, a unit's score the sum of its rows' (scaled to one unit of its count, its last row's), the Bonferroni cutoff over the units; both name the unit by its `fit_tvc` identifier. | proposed | A unit, not a row, is what is dropped or checked; the identity with the ordinary fit holds (tested). | proposed |
| The `functional_form` plot is per row (row martingale residual against the row's covariate value), not per unit. | proposed | Therneau and Grambsch (2000, 5.6): a unit's covariate can change, so it has no single value to plot against. | proposed |
| `common_shape` tests only factors that keep one level through each unit; one that changes within a unit is noted and skipped. | proposed | The AFT fit evaluates each unit's baseline once, so its shape is one per unit. | proposed |
| The AIC screens refit each candidate with its own `fit_tvc` on the rows as given (an AFT candidate that refuses delayed entry or gaps is left out with a note). | proposed | Same data, same likelihood construction; refitting with `fit` would drop an entry at 0 on a baseline below 0. | proposed |
| `calibration` is skipped for every `fit_tvc` model (now parametric as well as Cox), with the Cox family's reason. | proposed | A unit's predicted survival depends on its covariate path; the check used to treat each row as a unit. | proposed |
| A weighted `fit_tvc` model refuses up front ("diagnose the same call without w="). | proposed | A weighted fit's pseudo-likelihood cannot be compared between models (the ordinary family fails at `aic()` with `WeightedRefusal`); a plain refusal says so first. | proposed |
| `fit_tvc` (PH, AH, PO and AFT) now records `tvc_subject_ids` (Cox's name) and the rows it was given (`_tvc_fit_rows`), which diagnose() needs; a model without them refuses ("Refit it with fit_tvc"). | proposed | The parametric fits did not keep which unit each row belongs to. Nothing is serialised; the schema is unchanged. | proposed |

### Regressions: with_params and the life at fixed covariates (WP3 3b)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `covariance_names` of a parametric regression is `parameter_names` (distribution parameters, then coefficients or life-model parameters, fixed ones and an accelerated life model's life-parameter placeholder included); of the semi-parametric proportional odds and Lin-Ying additive hazards models, the coefficients. `_estimates()` is `params` in that order. | proposed | `covariance()` is already ordered so; names and rows stay one to one, as for `Parametric`. | done |
| `with_params` keeps the structure (distribution and knots, link or life model, formula and its DataFrame encoding, `center`, `fixed`, unit) and drops the data, the fit, the covariance, the likelihood, a penalty and weights; `maximum` is `"not applicable"` and the printout says "given parameters". | proposed | The shared contract; a penalty and weights belong to the fit, not the model. | done |
| The vector's entries for a fixed parameter (and the life-parameter placeholder) are ignored, the fixed value kept; an aliased coefficient stays `nan`. | proposed | A draw from `covariance()` has zero variance there anyway; a fixed parameter is part of the structure. | done |
| A parametric regression's `with_params` refuses a non-finite parameter or one outside its support, saying to draw on a scale that keeps it inside or to fit with `center=True`. | proposed | Normal draws of a poorly determined baseline scale fall below 0 (14 of 4000 on the Rossi Weibull PH at `Z = 0`); predicting `nan` silently is worse. | done |
| Semi-parametric PO: `with_params(beta)` re-profiles the baseline odds at `beta` from the fitted data (`_inner`, tol 1e-12); at the fitted `beta` it keeps the fitted baseline. It needs the data, so it refuses on a model restored from a dict or built by `with_params`. | proposed | The profile-likelihood baseline at `beta` is what the fit would give there; holding the fitted baseline would not be. Re-profiling at the estimate recovers the fit's baseline to 1e-7; the gradient in the baseline at a moved `beta` is below 1e-8 of the events. | done |
| Lin-Ying AH: `with_params(beta)` uses the baseline Lin and Ying's estimator gives at `beta`, `H0 = Lambda - beta' G` (exact: it is linear in `beta`), from the risk-set means of the fitted data, which the rebuilt model keeps, so it rebuilds again; a model restored from a dict refuses. At `beta = 0` it is the pooled Nelson-Aalen estimate to 1e-12. | proposed | Exact, cheap, and keeps chained rebuilds working. | done |
| A rebuilt semi-parametric model's `covariance()` / `standard_errors()` refuse; `to_dict` stores its covariance as `nan` (no schema change), so a reloaded one gives `nan` rather than refusing. | proposed | No schema bump; a `nan` covariance is already a "no covariance" state the readers know. | done |
| `at(Z)` returns one generic `LifeAtCovariates` for every regression with `sf`/`qf` at `Z` (parametric families, `CoxPH`, semi-parametric PO and AH), delegating each call; one row only (several refused, saying to call it per row). `cb` / `quantile_cb` / `rmst` / `mean` forward to the regression's where it has them and refuse otherwise. | proposed | One object, no per-class code; the bounds at `Z` are the regression's own (Wald, LR, bootstrap), so nothing new to validate. | done |
| `LifeAtCovariates` is a view, with no `to_dict`: it holds the regression and the row. | proposed | It has no numbers of its own; saving the regression and the row is the round trip. | done |
| `at(Z).random(n)` is `qf(U)` with `U` drawn as `Parametric.random` draws it (same stream for a seed), `inf` where a step curve never reaches `U`. The regressions' own `random(size, Z)` keep their convention, `U` the survival (`qf(1 - U, Z)`), unchanged, and the new semi-parametric PO / AH `random` follow it. | proposed | `at(Z)` is to stand in for a univariate model, draw for draw; changing the regressions' `random` would change every seeded draw users have. | done |
| AFT and PO `random()`: already implemented (inverse transform, `U` the survival) before this piece; nothing changed. | proposed | The planned item was written against an older version. | done |
| Not done here: `with_params` on `CoxPH` (its Breslow baseline depends on `beta` and the data), frailty, Buckley-James and Aalen models. | proposed | Out of this piece's files and scope; `at(Z)` works on `CoxPH`. | open |
| Evidence: at R's estimate, `with_params(...).at([2])` on lung (sex) matches flexsurv 2.3.2 `summary(newdata=)` (Weibull AFT `sf`/`hf`/`Hf`/`qf`/`mean`, Weibull PH `sf`/`hf`/`qf`) and survreg's predicted quantiles to 1e-9 (`scripts/reference/reference_r_regression_at.R`). 4000 covariance draws (seed 20261105) through `with_params`: the spread of log `Hf` (Weibull PH) and of the normal quantile of `ff` (LogNormal AFT) at a row is 1.005-1.025 and 1.009-1.017 times the Wald standard error behind `cb`; the draws' mean of log `Hf` sits 0.11-0.14 SE above the point (curvature of draws in the natural parameters, which the delta method ignores). | proposed | The validation the shared contract asks for. | done |

### Royston-Parmar with_params (WP3 3c)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `RoystonParmarModel.with_params(params)` takes the spline coefficients in the order of a new `covariance_names` (`gamma_0`, `gamma_1`, ..., equal to `parameter_names`: every coefficient is estimated and nothing else is) and keeps the fit's knots, scale and unit. Wrong length, shape or a non-finite value: `ValueError` naming the expected length and the names. | proposed | The shared `with_params` contract. The knots were placed from the fit's data and are part of the model; no basis orthogonalisation is used, so knots plus coefficients reproduce every function exactly (round trip bit for bit; flexsurv's `psurvspline` family at given coefficients and knots matched to 1e-12 on all three scales). | done |
| Coefficients whose spline falls anywhere in log time, or is not rising beyond a boundary knot, are refused with a `ValueError` giving the lowest slope and the time where it is, and saying to leave the draw out (and count it) or fit a smaller `df` if many are. Not allowed with a caveat. The lowest slope is found exactly (constant beyond the boundary knots, a quadratic between knots). | proposed | Such coefficients are not a survival distribution: the cumulative hazard would decrease (a negative density, an ambiguous `qf`) or the survival would not run from 1 to 0. The package has no other way to build a spline from coefficients; `MixtureModel.with_params` likewise refuses a draw with a negative weight. Observed rate (seed 20261105, 4000 draws): 8, 11 and 14 left out on Rossi at df=3 (hazard, odds, normal); 96 to 517 (2 to 13%) for n = 30 Weibull samples at df=4, where leaving them out conditions the draws, which the docstring points to via the smaller-df remedy. | proposed |
| A model built by `with_params` has no data, covariance or likelihood: `maximum` is `"not applicable"`, `covariance()` / `standard_errors()` / `cb` refuse saying it was built from coefficients, `neg_ll` / `aic` / `aic_c` / `bic` refuse as a `from_params` model's do, the `repr` says "Fitted: no (given coefficients)", and `to_dict` writes `_neg_ll` and `ic_n` as null (restored as such; no schema change). A weighted fit's rebuilt model is just the spline at those coefficients. | proposed | Contract points 1 and 5; `from_dict` used to read a missing likelihood as 0. | done |
| Validation: on Rossi (df=3, hazard), the 2.5% and 97.5% points of `sf(t)` at 10, 20, 35, 52 weeks over 4000 `with_params` draws are within 0.0013 of the `cb` Wald band (tolerance 0.003), and their standard deviation over the delta-method SE is 1.000 to 1.028 (tolerance 0.05); `with_params(refit.params)` equals a refit on a bootstrap sample with the same knots to the last bit. | proposed | Contract point 6. The seed and the 4000 draws were fixed before the results were seen; the tolerances are about three Monte Carlo standard errors (0.0009 for a 2.5% point, 0.011 for the SD ratio), set with the results in view. | done |

### diagnose() for Bayesian fits (WP6 6f)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The posterior predictive check uses two discrepancies: the realised Anderson-Darling discrepancy (the univariate family's statistic of `u = F(x \| theta)`, data and replicate at the same posterior draw; Gelman, Meng and Stern 1996) and Pearson's `sum (O - E)^2 / E` of the failures in up to 10 bins (one per 5 failures, at the observed failure times' quantiles; one bin, the number of failures, below 10 failures), `E` the replicates' mean. p = `(1 + k) / (1 + B)`, as the package's bootstrap p-values; `B = n_boot`, 1000 draws by default. | proposed | The AD statistic reuses the univariate family's and works with censoring and truncation; the bins catch a wrong number or timing of failures with few failures. | done |
| Replicates keep the observed design: each unit drawn within its truncation window; a censored unit censored at its own observed time, a failed one at a time drawn from the censoring KM beyond its failure (the univariate bootstrap's `ResampleDesign`, reused). Left- and interval-censored data get no PPC (rows warn, nan). | proposed | Fixed-time censoring is reproduced exactly and random censoring from the observed censoring times; the inspection times of interval data are not recorded. | done |
| The PPC is reported as it is, conservative: in 300 correct-model samples (Weibull, n = 40, a third randomly censored) the AD check rejected at 5% in 0/300 and the bins check in 3/300. Against 100 log-normal lifetimes fitted with a Weibull its power was 12% (AD) and 25% (bins) in 100 samples, the ML fit's bootstrap AD test's 90% (`scripts/studies/bayes_diagnose_power.py`). The calibration test asserts only that it rejects no more than nominally. A calibrated alternative (the sampled-posterior p-value, Gosselin 2011: the p-value at one posterior draw, uniform under the prior predictive) was not used because its verdict would change with the seed. | proposed | The standard PPP; its conservatism is documented in the module and the docs. The lead may prefer the calibrated variant. | open |
| The goodness-of-fit rows are the univariate family's bootstrap tests run on `model.mle` (reused, not copied), not at the posterior mean or mode. | proposed | The bootstrap's null distribution is that of a maximum-likelihood refit; the statistic at the posterior's centre would not be comparable with it. They test the distribution, which the prior does not change, and have more power than the PPC (see `scripts/studies/bayes_diagnose_power.py`). | done |
| Prior-data conflict: Box's (1980) prior predictive check in its normal form (Evans and Moshonov 2006): on each parameter's unconstrained scale, `z = (MLE - prior mean) / sqrt(V_prior + V_data)`, `V_data` the inverse of minus the log-likelihood's Hessian at the MLE; one row per parameter with a proper prior, plus a joint chi-square when there are two or more. Prior moments from the prior's own grid when every parameter has a proper prior, else from each single prior's draws (20000, fixed seed 0) and a MultivariateNormal's mean and covariance. Flat parameters and power priors are not checked; data with no MLE give rows that warn. | proposed | Cheap and general (no refits); exact for normal-normal; with the truth drawn from the prior it rejected at 5% in 4.7%, 3.7% and 3.0% (joint) of 300 samples. | done |
| Grid accuracy: a grid posterior is recomputed with `points = max(start, min(2m - 1, largest))` and the largest change of any free parameter's 2.5/50/97.5% points, as a fraction of the finer 95% width, warns at 1% and fails at 5%; an edge screen warns above 1e-6 of the mass in the outermost cells; a Laplace fit is compared with the grid posterior the same way; closed forms and `from_draws` have no rows (a note). The change row is a computed accuracy, not a test or a screen, so it can fail. | proposed | The engine's own target is 0.1% (0.5% in three dimensions); 1% is ten times that, and 5% changes what a summary reports. A deliberately coarse grid moved by 27%; the default grid by under 0.1%. | done |
| Advice: a failed PPC is blamed on the prior (advice `compare()` and a wider `prior.around`) when the prior-data conflict fails or the ML fit passes every goodness-of-fit test, else on the distribution (`fit_best`). | proposed | A prior far from the data makes the posterior fail the PPC while the distribution is right; advising other distributions then would mislead. | done |

### Parametric competing-risks uncertainty (WP3 3d)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ParametricCompetingRisks.params` is the causes' `params` concatenated in `causes` order, `parameter_names` is `"<cause>: <name>"`, and `covariance_names` is each cause's own `covariance_names` (with `lfp_p`, `f0`) prefixed the same way; `_estimates()` follows `covariance_names`. | proposed | The shared `with_params` contract: the short `params` keeps its meaning, the covariance names every row. | done |
| `covariance()` is block diagonal of the causes' `covariance()`; a cause without one (MPS/MSE/MPP, built from parameters, singular) raises `no_covariance_error` naming the cause. A cause's offset `gamma` has no row and is kept from the fit (`with_params(..., gamma={cause: value})` replaces it). | proposed | The likelihood factorises by cause, so the zeros are exact; offsets follow WP2a. | done |
| `with_params` rebuilds each `Parametric` cause by `dist.from_params(core, lfp_p=, f0=, gamma=<fitted>, unit=)` (any other cause by its own `with_params`) and returns `from_fitted` of them; it accepts the `covariance_names` length or the `parameter_names` length, else `ValueError` naming both. | proposed | Independent of `Parametric.with_params` changes in flight; values out of range are refused by `from_params`. | done |
| `cb(x, event=None, on="cif", alpha_ci, bound, method="wald", n_boot, random_state)` takes the nonparametric `CompetingRisks.cb` argument order; `on="cif"` without `event` refuses (as there), though `cif(x)` alone is the all-cause `ff`. | proposed | The two competing-risks models answer the same call the same way. | done |
| Wald scales: all-cause `sf`/`ff`/`Hf` share one bound on `log H` with `Var H = sum_k Var H_k` from each cause's autograd gradient; the CIF is bounded on the logit scale with a central-difference gradient (step `1e-5 * max(abs(value), se)`, one-sided where a step leaves the range). | proposed | The univariate transforms; the CIF is an integral. Checked: exponential CIF against its analytic gradient to 1e-6; analytic vs numerical `Var H` to 1e-6; coverage 0.936-0.962 over 27 cells (1000 replicates, MC se 0.006-0.008). | done |
| `cb(x, event=k, on="sf"|"ff"|"Hf")` is the cause's own `models[k].cb` (every `method`). `method="lr"`/`"bootstrap"` on the CIF or all-cause functions refuses, pointing to Wald or the nonparametric `CompetingRisks.cb`. | proposed | Net functions depend on one cause only. A likelihood-ratio band over all causes or a case bootstrap is possible but not built in this piece. | open |
| R `flexsurv` intervals for the CIF (`pmatrix.fs(ci=TRUE)`, percentiles of draws on the log scale) agree with the Wald bounds within 0.03 at 15 of 18 ends on a 40-unit set; the other three differ by 0.034-0.054 (a cause whose scale has a 53% SE). The test is kept as a strict xfail with these numbers. | proposed | Different methods; reported as found, not loosened. | open |

### Next-failure draws, state steps and with_params for recurrent models (WP3 3e)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The verbs are `next_failure_sf`, `next_failure_hf`, `next_failure_qf` and `next_failure_random` on both `RenewalModel` and `ParametricRecurrenceModel`, with the units given by `age=` (time since new) on both; an intensity model's events are minimal repairs, so its state is the age alone. | proposed | One spelling for a caller stepping either kind of component; `next_failure_sf/hf` already existed on the renewal side with `age=`. "Failure" is kept for the intensity models' events too, as the renewal side's name. | proposed |
| The public state is the existing `UnitStates` dataclass, made by `RenewalModel.states(age=None, *, failures=None, now=None)` and stepped by `RenewalModel.advance(states, x, failed=True)`, which returns new states (it does not change its argument); the next-failure methods take it as `states=`. `unit_states()` stays the table. `UnitStates` is not exported from `surpyval.recurrent`. | proposed | The state is already fully determined by each unit's failure times and time now, and every family's rule (Kijima I/II, ARA/ARI memory `m`, G1 scale) is already computed from those by `_state_of`, so `advance` appends a failure and recomputes: one implementation of each rule. Exporting the class would need a registry entry for a non-model. | proposed |
| `next_failure_random(size)` returns `(units, size)` and is `next_failure_qf` at `as_generator(random_state).random((units, size))`, the uniforms filling the result row by row. | proposed | Inverse transform as `Parametric.random` (common random numbers); `as_generator` as the recurrent simulators. | proposed |
| `next_failure_qf` works from the Exp(1) amount `-log1p(-p)`, through the samplers' own inversions (`conditional_gaps_from_energy`, factored out of `conditional_gaps`; `ari_gaps`, factored out of the ARI sampler, with two Newton polishing steps for `qf` only). `p = 0` gives 0, `p = 1` `inf`, outside [0, 1] `nan` with the package's warning. | proposed | Exact for small `p`, and the Newton/H inversion keeps the upper tail; the simulators' draws are unchanged. A time is exact only to the rounding of the age (an absolute ~1e-16 t): found and documented on the HPP at age 1e4 (relative error 1.6e-7 at p = 1e-6). | proposed |
| `covariance_names` is `parameter_names` on both (restoration parameter first on the renewal side); `_estimates()` is `params`; `with_params` takes exactly that flat vector, refuses any other shape with the length and names, and rebuilds through the family's `fit_from_parameters` / `from_params` (so their range checks apply), keeping kijima type, memory `m`, lifetime distribution or ARI baseline, and unit. The intensity side also refuses a value outside the process's `bounds`. | proposed | The shared WP3 contract; `from_params` of the NHPP fitters does not check ranges itself. | proposed |

## 2026-11-05

### The degradation level and `with_params` of the process models (WP3 3f)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| The level is `level_at(t, Z=None, *, y0=None, t0=0.0)`, returning a `ProcessLevel`. A reading is passed as `y0=` with its age `t0=`, not as `given=(t0, y0)`. | proposed | `y0=` already means "the level the unit starts from" on every process-model method, and `given=` elsewhere in the package is an age alone. `t0 > 0` without `y0` is refused. | done |
| `ProcessLevel` uses the package's names: `sf(y)` (above `y`), `ff(y)` (at or below), `df`, `qf`, `random`, `mean()`, `to_dict()`. `random` is `qf(U)`, as for `Parametric`. | proposed | Existing vocabulary; common random numbers across models. | done |
| `t` and `t0` are ages from new, and a `StepSchedule` `Z` runs from age 0 (as for `ff`). The level uses the clock time between them, `tau(t) - tau(t0)`. | proposed | The same time axis as the fit and the life; `predict_rul`'s schedule starts "now", which suits a remaining life but not a level at an age. | done |
| Any finite `y0` is taken by `level_at` and `random_path`, including one at or past the threshold. | proposed | The level process is defined there; the life methods still refuse such a start. | done |
| At `t = t0` the level is `y0` exactly: `sf`/`ff`/`qf` give the point mass and `df` refuses. `t < t0`, a non-finite `t` and several `t` at once are refused. | proposed | Refuse only where undefined, and say what to use (`random_path` for several times). | done |
| `with_params` takes only the full vector in `covariance_names` order (the process's two parameters, then the stress coefficients); a two-value vector for a stressed model is refused. | proposed | The process models had no `with_params`, so no shorter form is established; a silent "keep the fitted `gamma`" would hide a wrong-length draw. | done |
| `with_params` keeps `threshold`, `y0`, `stress_ref`, `Z_cols` and `unit`; the rebuilt model has `maximum = "not applicable"` and no covariance or likelihood. | proposed | The shared contract; `y0` is not in the covariance (it is not a parameter of the likelihood). | done |
| `GammaProcessModel(...)` refuses a non-positive or non-finite `alpha` or `beta`, and `WienerProcessModel(...)` a non-finite `mu`. | proposed | A covariance draw can produce them, and the models gave nonsense silently. | done |
| The process models have no frailty or random effects, so there is nothing further to apply to the level. | proposed | They pool the units' increments with shared parameters. | done |

### diagnose() for ParametricCompetingRisks (WP6 6h, 5 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Each cause's goodness of fit is the univariate family's check run on `models[cause]` (its fit with the other causes censored): the AD, KS and CvM statistics of `u = 1 - exp(-r)`, `r = H_k(x) - H_k(tl)` the Cox-Snell residual from entry, against the uniform, with the univariate parametric bootstrap (the cause's censoring includes the other causes' failures). Its rows, panels and AIC alternatives are the univariate ones, labelled by cause; each cause's bootstrap gets its own seed drawn from `random_state`. | proposed | Reuse, not a copy: one test of a distribution in the package. The likelihood factorises over causes, so the cause-specific fit is a univariate fit with the other causes as censoring, and the bootstrap's censoring distribution then carries them. | done |
| The incidence check is `D_k = sup_t abs(CIF_k(t) - AJ_k(t))` up to the last time observed, with a parametric-bootstrap p-value: latent times from every cause's model, the earliest kept, censored as the univariate bootstrap censors (Davison-Hinkley conditional), every cause refitted with its own options. Default refits: the univariate `DEFAULT_N_BOOT` (200). | proposed | A sup distance is the natural Kolmogorov-type comparison with a step estimate; the bootstrap gives its null with estimated parameters and censoring. | done |
| The bootstrap's parametric CIF is summed on the cause's probability scale with 8 Gauss-Legendre nodes per step (at the event times plus 100 grid points), not `model.cif`. | proposed | `cif`'s adaptive quadrature cost about 0.1 s a refit; the steps agree with it to 1e-7 (tested on Weibull shape 0.5, lognormal, gamma, lfp and offset causes). | done |
| With delayed entry the incidence rows are not computed (`p` NaN, verdict warn, a note says why); the goodness-of-fit rows allow for entry. | proposed | `CompetingRisks.fit` takes no `tl` on dev (an open PR adds it); passing `tl` to it here is a few lines once that merges. | superseded (2026-11-06): the incidence check now takes the entry times (see "Competing-risks diagnose(): delayed entry and joint fits") |
| A model restored with `from_dict`, assembled with `from_fitted` from fits of different units, or with a cause model that is not a `Parametric` refuses, saying what to do; a cause the univariate family refuses gets its message prefixed with the cause. The data are rebuilt from the causes' fits (unit counts by `(x, tl, tr)`), and the consistency of those counts across causes is the test of "same units". | proposed | `ParametricCompetingRisks` keeps no joint data, and its file is not this piece's to change. | done |
| Advice per cause: a better distribution by AIC -> `ParametricCompetingRisks.fit(dist={cause: ...})`; a mixture, offset, lfp or zi -> fit that cause alone and `from_fitted`; a failed goodness of fit no alternative explains -> the Aalen-Johansen estimate (`CompetingRisks.fit`) or, with covariates, cause-specific Cox; a failed incidence -> fix the causes whose goodness of fit fails first (an incidence depends on every cause's hazard), else the Aalen-Johansen estimate. | proposed | The univariate advice reworded for a per-cause model, naming only features that exist. | done |
| Size and power (`tests/calibration/test_size_parametric_competing_risks_diagnostics.py`, seeds 289 and 290, 200 replicates, n = 100, 49 refits, judged at 10%, MC se 0.021): right models, AD rejected 0.095 (a) and 0.070 (b), incidence 0.080 (a) and 0.105 (b); b fitted with a 2-parameter Weibull when it is 4 + an exponential, b's AD rejected 0.795 and its incidence 0.820, a's AD 0.055 (a's incidence 0.125, not judged: it depends on b's survival). | proposed | Seeds, sizes and targets fixed before the run. | done |

### Parametric competing risks with left- and interval-censored rows (WP5 5d)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ParametricCompetingRisks.fit` takes `c = -1` and `c = 2` rows with their cause. A window `(a, b]` of cause `k` contributes `CIF_k(b) - CIF_k(a)`, a left-censored row the window from its entry `tl` (or the start), each over `S(tl)` with entry. With any such row the causes are fitted jointly (one MLE over every cause's parameters); without one the fit is cause by cause, bit for bit as before (checked on 15 data sets, with entry, counts and per-cause families: parameters, inverse Hessians, `neg_ll`, `cb` and `to_dict` identical). | proposed | The window terms are integrals of the all-cause survival, so the likelihood no longer factorises; the factorised path stays the exact MLE where it is one. | proposed |
| The window integral is Gauss-Legendre quadrature on the cause's own probability scale (as `cif` integrates), with panels refined until the integral at reference parameters holds to 1e-12, graded in half decades towards `p = 0` for a window from the start of the support (where a shape's derivative has a `log t` singularity) and towards `p = 1` in the far tail. The nodes are held fixed so autograd gives the exact gradient and Hessian; the fit refines them at its answer and searches again until it no longer moves (BFGS, then Newton). | proposed | Fixed nodes on the time axis (log or linear) were off by up to 1e-2 for sharp hazards; on the probability scale the integrand is the other causes' survival, bounded by 1. The window probability matches `cif` differences to 1e-8 over 4 family pairs and 7 windows (worst 2e-9); the first grading (to 1e-8 only) left the shape gradient of a left-censored window 1e-5 off, found by the single-cause test against the univariate fit. | proposed |
| `covariance()` of a joint fit is the inverse of the joint Hessian, with terms between causes; each cause's model in `models` is its distribution `from_params` at the joint estimate, marked `"MLE"`, carrying its block of the covariance (so its net Wald `cb` is right) and no data. The all-cause `cb` (`sf`, `ff`, `Hf`) of a joint fit takes the gradient over every parameter by central differences (the cause-by-cause path keeps its analytic per-cause sum); `cif` bounds already used the full covariance. `cb(event=k, on="sf", method="lr"/"bootstrap")` refuses on a joint fit, saying the cause's own refit is not this likelihood and to use `method="wald"`. `neg_ll`, `aic`, `bic`, `aic_c`, `log_likelihood` are the joint fit's; BIC's `n` counts every failure (exact, left, interval). | proposed | WP3's block-diagonal covariance was exact only because the likelihood factorised. A joint likelihood-ratio or bootstrap band is possible but not built here. | proposed |
| `to_dict` of a joint fit adds an optional `"joint"` entry (covariance, `neg_ll`, `k`, `n`) beside the per-cause dicts; no schema change. A SurPyval before 0.27 reads such a dict as per-cause models with their blocks only (no terms between causes). | proposed | The brief rules out a schema bump; the per-cause blocks are still each cause's correct marginal covariance, so the older reading is approximate only for the CIF and all-cause bounds. The maintainer may prefer to stamp such dicts with a schema older readers refuse. | changed (2026-11-06): the maintainer chose schema 4 for joint fits (see "Competing-risks diagnose(): delayed entry and joint fits") |
| A window needs its cause: a failure in a window from an unknown cause is refused, saying to give the cause or drop the row (its contribution, `S(a) - S(b)`, would be easy, but an exact failure of unknown cause is refused too). Only `how="MLE"` and continuous `ParametricFitter` distributions; a window `[xl, inf]` is a right-censored row (the handler's reading), whose cause is refused with a message saying so. Without `c`, the flags of `[xl, xr]` rows are derived from the times (2, or -1 with no lower end), as every fit derives them; `fit_from_df` takes `xl_col`/`xr_col` with `x_col=None`. | proposed | Masked causes are a separate feature; refusals say what to use (principles 2 and 22). | proposed |
| The non-parametric `CompetingRisks.fit` keeps refusing `c = -1`/`2`, now with a message that names `ParametricCompetingRisks` (it used to say "not implemented", or report a shape mismatch). | proposed | No Aalen-Johansen estimate for interval data is in scope. | proposed |
| Evidence: no R package fits this model (flexsurv, cmprsk, mstate take exact times; icenReg has no competing risks; intccr is not installed and is semiparametric), so the reference is the likelihood written out in base R (`integrate`, `optim`, numDeriv's Hessian; `scripts/reference/reference_r_parametric_competing_risks_interval.R`): parameters to 1e-5 (R's own optimum is flat along a scale with a 57% SE; ours is within 5e-8 of it), `neg_ll` to 1e-7 (agrees to 3e-11), covariance to 1e-3 (agrees to 3e-5), CIFs to 2e-6. Study (`scripts/studies/parametric_competing_risks_interval_coverage.py`, 500 replicates, seeds fixed first): with inspections every 2 every coverage cell is 0.940 to 0.964; every 5, cause `b`'s CIF at t = 4 (inside the first window) covers 0.922 (2.8 MC SE low) and the other cells 0.932 to 0.958. Calibration test (200 replicates, seed 20261107, inspections every 2): 0.930 to 0.975 within its 0.056 tolerance. | proposed | Seeds, sample sizes and tolerances were fixed before the results were seen; the gap5 shortfall is reported as found. | proposed |

### Joint fits that reach a correlation of +-1 (12 Nov 2026)

| Decision | By | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| A joint fit whose search ends with a correlation between the random effects within 5e-7 of +-1 (a Cholesky diagonal below 1e-3 of its row's length) searches once more from the same sds with no correlation, and keeps that answer only where its negative log-likelihood is lower. Every other fit is unchanged to the last digit. | proposed | The log-scale Cholesky diagonal reaches the edge only at -inf, where the gradient vanishes, so the search can stop on that flat shelf short of a maximum inside. Two of 400 calibration replicates (seeds 7300 and 7728) did, 38.8 and 37.1 below the maximum, with corr_a_b 1 against 0.22 and 0.23 inside. A true edge maximum returns to the edge and is kept. | done |
| Revised the same day: the second search runs whenever the first answer is not a verified maximum (any `q >= 2` fit), not only within 5e-7 of +-1, and its answer is kept where it is verified, else the lower of the two. A verified first answer gets no second search, so is unchanged to the last digit. | proposed | Where the first search stops depends on the last bits of the arithmetic: the replicate's test failed on Linux x86-64 (numpy 2.4) only. With its readings changed by 1e-11 of themselves, 3 of 132 such fleets stopped where the adaptive nodes gave 5060.8 to 5099.8, against the maximum's 5159.0; a grid integral at one gives 5197.8 (the nodes are not accurate for a spread so near rank one), so comparing likelihoods rejected the maximum. A point whose Hessian is not positive definite is not a maximum, whatever its value. | done |
